| D-154 | 2026-10-19 | **`fold_confirm.grade_test` reads the claim's floor and counts "all but one" over the fold's windows (R5); the `past_return` reversal bias is recorded as a known property. No flag.** **Ruling (operator, 2026-10-19, report #495, item 1, verbatim):** "R5 approved as a fix PR, lane 2 now: fold_confirm.grade_test reads the claim's floor as claim_measure computes it (any floor_not_met entry at any horizon: not_measurable, reason "floor not met"); all-but-one counted over the fold's windows, an empty window counts against; fewer windows with a value than the fold's windows minus 1: not_measurable; not_confirmed only for a real refutation (wrong sign or non-positive pooled value). D-090 unchanged; the three recorded confirmations unchanged; no record touched. Opus for grade_test, Sonnet for tests and docs. In the same PR: a paragraph in CLAIM_TESTS.md under past_return on the reversal bias (about twice the documented chance on an event selector, less on quantile; cite the report). One D-row: R5 plus the bias as a known property." **What changed (`tools/fold_confirm.py`):** (1) the floor: any entry in the measured result's `floor_not_met` (the list `claim_measure` builds, pooled per horizon over the fold's windows, `min_events` / `min_windows` / `min_blocks` / `min_eras`) at any horizon makes the test `not_measurable` with a reason that starts "floor not met", checked before any count, so a floor not met is never `confirmed` and never a refutation, even at 0 of 6. (2) the count: `grade_test(result, fold_windows=None)` takes the fold's window labels; `confirm_on_fold` passes them from `fold_blocks` (`block_rows`), so a window with no bars or no event counts against exactly like a window with the wrong sign. A horizon is judged only when at least (fold windows minus `WINDOWS_ALLOWED_AGAINST`) of the fold's windows have a value; fewer is `not_measurable` ("too few of the fold's windows with a value to grade"), not `not_confirmed`. A judged horizon fails (`not_confirmed`) only through a window with a value of the wrong sign (or a zero) beyond the allowance, or a non-positive pooled value, and a refutation at a judged horizon stands whatever a thinner horizon says. Without `fold_windows` the count runs over the windows the measurement carries (a cell with no value still counts against; a window with no cell cannot be seen). (3) `MIN_WINDOWS = 4` is kept as a lower bound: windows needed with a value are `max(MIN_WINDOWS, fold windows minus WINDOWS_ALLOWED_AGAINST)`; on every fold of `config/folds.yaml` (6 blocks) that is 5, so it has no separate role there and only backstops a fold of 4 blocks or fewer (folds below 4 blocks were not measured). (4) the row's `agreement` is "k of F" (F = the fold's windows, not the windows with a value), and each horizon carries `windows_in_fold`, `windows_against`, `windows_without_value` and `windows_needed_with_value`; `RULE_TEXT` and the module docstring state R5. **Measured rates (REPORT.md section 4.2, 2000 sims per cell, SE about 0.007 at 0.11):** a null fold with 6 full windows 0.115-0.117, unchanged; thin selectors 0.099-0.117 to 0.057-0.102; 1 of 6 windows empty 0.19-0.20 to 0.03-0.04 (0.025-0.039); 2 of 6 empty 0.27-0.29 to 0.000. Power: no cost when every window has a value and the floor is met; about 0.09 at a 2% real effect on a fold of about 115 events against a floor of 100; power with empty windows not measured and lower by construction. **Records:** the three recorded confirmations (`forecast_power-run_095-1`, `trade_efficiency-run_066-2`, `trade_efficiency-run_071-4`) stay `confirmed` when re-graded under R5 (REPORT.md section 3: each has 6 windows with events and at most one window against at any horizon (`trade_efficiency-run_066-2`, child run_079: 5 of 6 at h1, 6 of 6 at h6 and h24), and clears its own floor `min_events: 100, min_windows: 4`); no record, run folder or `campaign_record/confirmations.yaml` row is touched. **D-090 is unchanged:** a claim confirmed on any fold stays confirmed. **Known property, not fixed here: the `past_return` reversal bias.** On a pure random walk a `past_return` reversal claim clears the fold rule far more often than the matching momentum claim: `event` cut-off 0.230 (`less` after a rise of 5% over 5 bars) and 0.217 (`greater` after the matching fall), against 0.041 and 0.050 for momentum, with the symmetric chance about 0.11; `quantile` cut-off 0.135 against 0.079 (REPORT.md section 2.2). R5 does not remove it (0.230 / 0.041 under R5). Cause: a selected bar's forward move is part of the `past_return` of the bars after it, so the selected mean is biased low (REPORT.md section 2.4); spacing candidates at least bars + h apart removed it as a control (0.502 per-window share) and was not built. `workflow_artifacts/skills/hypothesis-design/CLAIM_TESTS.md` now carries a guidance paragraph under the field definitions (advice only, no refusal, no new table row). **Remedy ordered by the operator: a second measurement report, no code.** **Reports re-run:** the scripts in `engineering/FOLD-FLOORS-2026-10-19/` (`fold_floor_sim.py`, `regrade.py`) call `grade_test` as "R0"; re-running them after this change gives R5 numbers under that label (without `fold_windows`, the count runs over the cells the measurement carries). **Tests:** `tests/test_e077_pr2_confirm.py`: three tests changed to the R5 statuses and reasons (4 of 6 windows with a value is `not_measurable`; a coin with bars in 3 or 4 of 6 windows likewise, 5 of 6 confirms), and new tests for the floor path (including a 0 of 6 refutation and a floor entry at another horizon), 5 measured all positive confirms, 4 positive / 1 negative / 1 absent is `not_confirmed`, 5 positive / 1 absent with a negative pooled value is `not_confirmed`, 4 positive / 1 None / 1 absent is `not_measurable`, "k of F" agreement, the count without `fold_windows`, a refuting judged horizon beside a short one, and synthetic shapes of the three recorded confirmations (no run data). **Ruling (operator, 2026-10-19 evening, item 2, verbatim, added to the R5 PR before its review closed):** "R5 / D-154 approved, with one requirement added to the R5 PR before its review closes: a claim graded not_measurable must keep what was measured. [...] Smallest fix, all additive: (a) per-window cells gain n_events (selected events with an outcome in that window), bar and trade paths; (b) grade_test copies them into a sibling key events_per_window beside per_window (row shape unchanged); (c) each test entry in the row gains the claim's floor (claim_measure's `floor` dict); (d) the memory view's per-fold entries gain `reason` and `agreement` for every status; effects stay confirmed-only in the view; update the NOTE text. Early exits before measurement keep status and reason only, as now." **Built (keep-measured):** (a) `n_events` per window cell in `tools/claim_tests.py`: bar path, trade path (including `post_exit_return` after its dedup), and for several coins pooled per window the sum over the coins, so the cells sum to the pooled `n_events`; (b) `fold_confirm.grade_test` copies them into `events_per_window` beside `per_window` (same keys, row shape otherwise unchanged); (c) each test entry in the row carries the claim's `floor` (a copy of `claim_measure`'s `floor` dict, via `row_test_entry`), whatever the status; (d) the memory view (`tools/analyst_memory_view.py`) shows, for every status, per-fold entries `{fold, run_id, status, reason (numbers masked), agreement ("k of F", keyed by spec_hash)}`; effects stay confirmed-only; the NOTE text is updated, and the section heading in `tools/analyst_session.py` now reads "effect numbers only for confirmed ones". **Declared prompt change:** the memory view and its NOTE are part of the analyst prompt, so the analyst prompt text changes with this PR. Early exits before measurement (no tests, bars or source unreadable, not comparable) keep status and reason only. **The three recorded confirmations, re-graded independently: unchanged** (events per window: run_096 53/52/55/58/59/53; run_078 502/556/437/606/663/597; run_079 h1 321/363/365/380/380/386). | E-077 (folds, PR-2); D-087; D-089; D-090; FOLD-FLOORS-2026-10-19 | operator ruling, 2026-10-19 (report #495, item 1); `strategy-research/engineering/FOLD-FLOORS-2026-10-19/REPORT.md` | `strategy-research/tools/fold_confirm.py` (`MIN_WINDOWS`, `RULE_TEXT`, `grade_test`, `confirm_on_fold`); `strategy-research/workflow_artifacts/skills/hypothesis-design/CLAIM_TESTS.md`; `strategy-research/tests/test_e077_pr2_confirm.py` (keep-measured: `strategy-research/tools/claim_tests.py` (`n_events` per window); `strategy-research/tools/analyst_memory_view.py` (`NOTE`, folds entries); `strategy-research/tools/analyst_session.py` (memory-view heading); `strategy-research/docs/USER_GUIDE.md`; `strategy-research/tests/test_d089_vehicle_and_coins.py`, `test_e075_pr4_queries.py`) | built (no flag) |
| D-155 | 2026-10-19 | *(PR-C of lane 1. Operator rulings 2026-10-19: B1, B2, S1, N1. No flag; every config, claim and fixture that does not use the new pieces is byte-identical.)* **Claim tests gain `all_of`, `abs_forecast`, `forecast_sign_change` and `trade_gross_return`; D-095 is extended to the lot's own results; `trade_cost_share` is dropped.** **`all_of` (`tools/claim_tests.py`: `sel_all_of`, `_check_all_of`, `_canon_all_of`):** `{kind: all_of, selectors: [...]}` selects the bars where 2 to 3 bar selectors hold together: mask = the AND of the masks, valid = the AND of the valids (a bar is in the baseline only where every part is defined). Rules: exactly the keys `kind` and `selectors`; 2 to 3 parts; no nesting (an `all_of` inside an `all_of` is refused; list every condition in one); no `all` part (it adds nothing); no part twice (compared by canonical JSON); each part checked as a selector of its own. Allowed as the selector and as an `other_selector` baseline's selector. **Canonical part order in `spec_hash`:** the parts are sorted by their canonical JSON before hashing, so the order the author wrote them in does not change the hash (AND is commutative); only `all_of` is touched, so every other spec hash is unchanged. **Regime parts give an effect size only:** a `regime` or `regime_change` part sets `verdict_possible` false, in the selector and in the baseline, as such a selector alone does. A calendar part keeps its period for the placebo shifts (a week if any calendar part names weekdays, else a day). **Why `all_of` cannot leak:** the AND of past-only masks reads nothing a part does not read. **`abs_forecast` and `forecast_sign_change` (bar-t fields, `event` and, for `abs_forecast`, `quantile`; no `bars`):** `abs_forecast` is `abs(forecast[t])`, row t only, NaN where the forecast is NaN. `forecast_sign_change` is 1.0 when `forecast[t-1]` and `forecast[t]` are of strictly opposite signs, else 0.0 (a zero forecast has no sign: + then 0 and 0 then - are not changes); NaN where the bar stamped exactly `ts[t] - step` is not in the same window (every window's first bar, a gap) or either forecast is not finite; the same definition as the analyst's derived column (`analyst_queries.DERIVED_COLUMNS`, tested equal). **Why neither can leak:** they read the forecast saved at the close of bar t (and of bar t - 1 for the second), never a later bar, and nothing across windows (tested by changing later rows and checking earlier values do not move). **N1: a `quantile` selector on `forecast_sign_change` is refused** with "forecast_sign_change is 0 or 1: select it with {kind: event, field: forecast_sign_change, op: "==", value: 1}" (a trailing quantile of a 0/1 series is not a useful cut); `abs_forecast` stays allowed in `quantile`. **`trade_gross_return` (gated trade family, `TRADE_OWN_OUTCOMES` = `trade_net_return`, `trade_gross_return`):** the lot's return before costs, a fraction, on the window's return basis, with no horizons (horizon 0, the lot's life), graded like `trade_net_return` (`min_events` counts lots, a block is a stretch of overlapping trades, the basis is recorded per window, windows with different bases are not pooled: `not_measured`, `mixed_return_basis`). On the two bases net = gross - cost: `all_costs` gross = `gross_return_before_costs` / 100 (at the bar closes), `net_of_fees_and_slippage` gross = `profit_loss_percent` / 100 (at the fill prices, before commission); a missing number makes the lot's value empty. Known only at the exit: an outcome, never a selector field. **S1: `trade_slice` reads the same number.** `analyst_queries` `trade_slice` `trade_gross_return` is now the TradeWindow's per-lot gross (the claim outcome's own values), on the window's basis, and the reply's `return_basis` names the basis; before this it was always `trades.json` `profit_loss_percent` / 100, which differs from the claim outcome on an `all_costs` window. `trade_cost_pct` is unchanged (the commission rate, `total_commission_percent`, as documented). `docs/DATA_DICTIONARY.md` section 10 and the `_trades_json_values` docstring say so. **D-095 extension (B1):** the analyst's checker (`analyst_session.check_answer`) now refuses a lot selected by `holding_bars` or `exit_cause` and measured by either of the lot's own results (`trade_net_return` or `trade_gross_return`), and the message names the outcome found; `post_exit_return` stays allowed. The guide example `reductions_cost_more_of_the_move` is dropped and replaced in `CLAIM_TESTS_TRADE.md` by an entry-time selector: `where: [entry_forecast > -10, entry_forecast < 10]`, outcome `trade_gross_return`, baseline `other_trades`, `mean_diff`, direction `less`, floor `{min_events: 100, min_windows: 4}` (run through `check_claim` with `trade_tests=True` by `tests/test_pr_c_claim_tests.py`, together with the other two examples). The analyst skill's step 3 now ends "claim it with all_of." **B2: `trade_cost_share` is dropped as a claim outcome** (it existed only on this branch before the ruling; master never had it). Reason (operator, 2026-10-19): the per-lot share is dominated by lots with a near-zero gross; the hit-rate DIFFERENCE is zero by construction: both groups sit near 1.0 on a non-negative outcome; fold_confirm needs every test confirmed (fold_confirm.py:295), so a descriptive test cannot sit inside a claim. The cost share is evidence read from trade_slice (mean gross minus mean net of the selected lots, window's basis) and window_economics (cost_drag_pct). `CLAIM_TESTS.md` `cost_turnover` row now reads "test one: the cause (horizon_decay, direction_forecast by horizon, conditional_behaviour on abs_forecast, or event on forecast_sign_change); the cost share is evidence from trade_slice and window_economics, not a test."; the sentence under the kinds table is removed; a `trade_cost_share` outcome is refused as an unknown trade outcome by `check_spec`, `check_claim` and `conditional_effect` (tested). **Existing spec hashes and fixtures unchanged:** no fixture file is in the branch diff against origin/master; no pinned spec-hash literal in any existing test changed (the diff to existing tests is only the field-list, selector-list and count pins, plus new `all_of` fixtures), and `_canon_all_of` returns any selector that is not a well-formed `all_of` as it came, so no existing spec hashes differently. **Measured null rates for the new bar pieces (safety review of this PR; synthetic run_097-like host, 2000 folds per cell, no effect planted):** `forecast_sign_change == 1`, `less` / `greater`: 0.106 / 0.121 at h1 and 0.114 / 0.104 at h3; `abs_forecast` quantile top 20%: about 0.11; `all_of` adds no bias by itself. Documented, not gated: no code path uses these rates. | E-068 (claim tests); E-075; D-095; D-097; D-153 | operator rulings 2026-10-19 (PR-C spec; decisions B1, B2, S1, N1) | `strategy-research/tools/claim_tests.py` (`sel_all_of`, `_check_all_of`, `_canon_all_of`, `abs_forecast`, `forecast_sign_change`, `TRADE_OWN_OUTCOMES`, `trade_outcome`); `strategy-research/tools/claim_card.py`; `strategy-research/tools/claim_measure.py`; `strategy-research/tools/analyst_queries.py` (`_trade_values`, `_trades_json_values`); `strategy-research/tools/analyst_session.py` (`check_answer`); `strategy-research/workflow_artifacts/skills/hypothesis-design/CLAIM_TESTS.md`; `strategy-research/workflow_artifacts/skills/hypothesis-design/CLAIM_TESTS_TRADE.md`; `strategy-research/workflow_artifacts/skills/analyst/SKILL.md`; `strategy-research/docs/DATA_DICTIONARY.md`; `strategy-research/tests/test_pr_c_claim_tests.py`, `test_window_economics.py`, `test_e075_pr5_analyst.py`, `test_e068_claim_tests.py`, `test_e074_s1_trailing_vol.py`, `test_e075_pr3_trade_tests.py`, `test_pr_a_vehicle_observations.py` | built (no flag) |

The `past_return` rate paragraph in `CLAIM_TESTS.md` is replaced by a general statement of the overlap effect, and `CLAIM_TESTS_TRADE.md` gains one line on market drift. Wording only; no code, no re-test.** **Ruling (operator, 2026-10-20, item 4, verbatim):** "Item 4, operator decision: option A, no rule change. One docs PR (Sonnet) after #499, wording only, no re-test: - D-row: quotes the ruling; cites #495 and #499 for the measured rates (the rates live in the D-row and the reports, not in the guides); notes that the R5 guidance paragraph's "simulated, not measured on market data" is superseded by #499 part (b); one line: a per-episode event selector is the correction to build if a claim of this shape ever reaches confirmation. - CLAIM_TESTS.md: replace the whole "Guidance for past_return" paragraph (master :131-146, rates, quantile preference and source line included) with: "A test can pass by chance more often in one direction than the other when the rule that selects events depends on the same moves the outcome measures: the selection keeps running or stops according to what happens next, so overlapping events share the moves they are graded on. The chance rate is then no longer the documented one: it favours one direction and penalises the opposite. The favoured direction is the one in which the claimed outcome ends the selection; a confirmation against it is stronger evidence, not weaker. When you design a claim, prefer selections whose events do not share their outcome window, even at a cost in events; when you cannot, say so in the rationale and treat its confirmation with more doubt." The trailing_vol guidance below it stays. - CLAIM_TESTS_TRADE.md: add one line: "A claim that compares groups of lots on their own return also measures what the market did over the windows (its drift, through the sides the groups hold and how long their lots stay open); state in the rationale how the claim separates the strategy's effect from the market's." Keep the three examples as they stand after #498. - No other example in either guide illustrates these points; remove none. - Tests: the guide-text pins in the R5 and PR-C test files that quote the old paragraph, updated; nothing else." **What changed:** (1) `CLAIM_TESTS.md`: the whole paragraph that began "Guidance for `past_return` (nothing refuses this; it is advice)" (D-154) is replaced by the ruling's text; the "Guidance for `trailing_vol`" block below it is byte-identical. (2) `CLAIM_TESTS_TRADE.md`: the ruling's one line is added as a paragraph after the slot-semantics list (the bullet on the missing p-value), before the closed field list; the three examples are untouched. No rate, no quantile preference and no source line stays in either guide. **Pins:** no test quoted the old paragraph (a search of `strategy-research/tests` for its distinctive phrases found none), so no test changed. **Measured no-edge confirm rates (the grade is `fold_confirm.grade_test` under R5 on a fold of 6 windows; symmetric chance 7/64 = 0.109; SE in brackets; kept here and in the reports, not in the guides).** From #495, `strategy-research/engineering/FOLD-FLOORS-2026-10-19/REPORT.md`, section 2.2 (simulated random walk with no edge, 2000 sims per cell; the R5 column of its section 4.2 repeats cell A as 0.230 / 0.041): `past_return(5) >= +5%` event cut-off, `less` (reversal) 0.230 (0.009) and `greater` (momentum) 0.041 (0.004), per-window share with the claimed sign 0.596 / 0.404; `past_return(5) <= -5%`, `greater` (reversal) 0.217 (0.009) and `less` (momentum) 0.050 (0.005); `quantile` top 20% of the last 20 bars, `less` 0.135 (0.008) and `greater` 0.079 (0.006); `trailing_vol(10) >= 0.035`, `less` 0.114 (0.007) and `greater` 0.107 (0.007), the symmetric family. From #499, `strategy-research/engineering/FOLD-FLOORS-2/REPORT.md`: section 3.4 (part (b), cell A on real returns: sign-shuffled 5-bar blocks of the campaign coins' daily returns, 7 coin-folds, 14000 fold-sims), `less` after a rise 0.1954 (0.0034), 2736 of 14000, and `greater` after a rise 0.0450 (0.0018), 630 of 14000, per-window share 0.5884 / 0.4116, and all 7 coin-folds lean toward `less` (shares 0.519 to 0.645); the same section gives the synthetic tool null as 0.5972 / 0.212 / 0.032 (share / `less` / `greater`); section 2.2 (part (a), trade-level null, synthetic hosts, 2000 folds per cell, claimed direction first): T1 (`side == short`, `less`) on the d155 host 0.2315 (0.0094) against 0.0455 (0.0047) under log0 and 0.0900 against 0.1140 under arith0, on the lifo host 0.2210 against 0.0390 under log0 and 0.1060 against 0.1085 under arith0; T3 (`entry_forecast >= 10`, `greater`) on the d155 host 0.1795 (0.0086) against 0.0640 under log0 and 0.1245 against 0.0890 under arith0, on the lifo host 0.0690 against 0.1470 under log0 and 0.0390 against 0.2265 under arith0 (against the claim); T2 (`exit_cause == flip`, `post_exit_return`) not measurable on the d155 host and 0.0035 to 0.0065 on the lifo host. No flag.** **The fault:** `strategies/regime_engine.py` says detector components (`regime_detector.components[j]`) have no transforms, and the engine never reads those lists, so a config that added one passed every check while the run measured an unchanged strategy. **Where it was found:** the PR-E Opus safety review of #494, by reading the code and on a constructed config; it was not seen in any real config (a 2026-10-20 search of the committed configs found none). **Ruling (operator, 2026-10-20, item 5, verbatim):** "5. Detector-component transforms: yes, smallest form. Complete validate_config._all_transform_lists to cover regime_detector.components[*], with your message. Before wiring, state whether the analyst answer read and step 1b call validate_config; if both do, add no separate refusal site. Every existing config byte-identical (you found none affected). Sonnet, one review round, D-row. Own PR, before the vehicle-format work; V1 then inherits it (Q6)." **Ruling (operator, 2026-10-20, item 5 confirmed, verbatim):** "5. Confirmed: no separate refusal site. validate_config only, as you described (_all_transform_lists yields the detector lists; one new check refuses them with your message). D-158." **The rule:** `validate_config` only. It runs before every backtest: `AdvancedStrategy` construction calls `validate` and raises `ValueError("invalid strategy_config")` (`main_strategy.py`, checked and tested here), and `run_loop` (`workflow/run_phase1_research.py:20332`) runs `tools/validate_config.py` on `ARTIFACTS/candidate_strategy_config.json` after variant selection and before the backtest (`:20934-20939`; a nonzero exit sets `failed_validation`), and the per-variant check at `:2977-2981` does the same for each variant config, so the refusal arrives before any bar is read. There is no separate refusal site. The analyst-reading refusal arrives with vehicle-format V1, which inherits this rule (Q6). **What changed (`trading-bot/tools/validate_config.py`):** (1) `_all_transform_lists` also yields `regime_detector.components[j].transforms` and `.history_transforms` (non-empty lists only; a non-dict entry or a `null` `components` is skipped). (2) New check **V17**, one line per non-empty list, location first: `VIOLATION V17 <location>: detector components take no transforms (the engine ignores them: regime_engine.py); put the transform on a veto, on a score-mode regime component, or on a strategy component`. It covers the top-level detector and, through V16's block-source walk, a block's source detector (`strategies.regimes.<r>.blocks[i].source.regime_detector.components[j]...`). Vetoes, score-mode regime components (`regime_detector.regimes.<r>.components`) and strategy components apply their transforms and are not touched; an empty or absent list draws nothing. (3) Because the lists are now yielded, the checks that walk `_all_transform_lists` (V3, V4, V5, V14, V15, V16) also report on such a config, in addition to V17; the V16 comment that said `_all_transform_lists` is unchanged is updated. **Every existing config validates byte-identically:** `validate()` was run on 1063 committed configs (1025 JSON files with a `regime_detector` key, plus 38 YAML `config:` blocks; 95 of the 1063 have both top-level keys, among them `trading-bot/strategy_config.json`, the 3 files in `trading-bot/tests/fixtures`, `strategy-research/config/regime_retune_winner.json`, the 3 `strategy_config*.json` under `strategy-research/tests/fixtures` and the 38 `config:` blocks of `strategy-research/runs/*/artifacts/backtest_spec.yaml` that hold a config) with master's `validate_config.py` (loaded from a temp copy) and with this branch's: 0 differ, and 0 has a detector component transform. `run_protocol.json`, `local_data` and the holdout store were not read. **Tests:** `trading-bot/tests/test_validate_config_detector_transforms.py` (17 tests: the exact line and location for `transforms`, `history_transforms`, both on one component, a second component, and a block's source detector; the same transform on a veto, a score-mode regime component and a strategy component draws no V17 line; an empty or `null` list draws none; a malformed `components` entry does not raise; V3 and V16 now also report on such a list; `AdvancedStrategy` construction refuses the config and accepts the same config without the list). `strategy-research/docs/STRATEGY_DESIGN_GUIDE.md` lists V17 in the validator table. | PR-E (#494); vehicle-format V1 (Q6) | operator ruling, 2026-10-20 (item 5, both texts above) | `trading-bot/tools/validate_config.py` (`_all_transform_lists`, V17, V16 comment); `trading-bot/tests/test_validate_config_detector_transforms.py`; `strategy-research/docs/STRATEGY_DESIGN_GUIDE.md` | built (no flag) |

| D-160 | 2026-10-20 | **Vehicle-format V1d: a config vehicle that combines as a regime gate must have the gate form, and code derives its ungated twin (D-052); `validate_config` gains V18 (a default regime with no strategy). No flag.** **Operator rulings, 2026-10-20 (verbatim; line breaks flattened to spaces):** (1) "- V1d: code derives the ungated twin, as its own PR after V1 and before V2 (order V1, V1d, V2, V3). Form: combines_as regime_gate with a config vehicle requires non-empty rules or vetoes and exactly one non-null strategies regime, else refused with a message. Twin by the D-052 recipe: rules emptied, vetoes dropped, default_regime set to that regime, detector components kept; validated when read like the config. The null-default-regime gap: search every config, run spec and results manifest as for D-158; none affected -> a general validate_config check (V18); one affected -> the check is twin-only and the finding comes to the operator. Opus core, Opus safety review, Sonnet tests; D-row." (2) "- V18: confirmed, threshold_rules mode only (rules empty or missing, default regime null or missing in strategies.regimes); refuses nothing that exists today. V1d starts after 503 merges, as ruled (order V1, V1d, V2, V3)." (3) "1. V1d, D-160: (a) yes, V18 does not fire when a veto's result names a trading regime; (b) yes, the gate form requires threshold_rules mode and refuses score and score_product with a message; (c) yes, V18 also checks a block's source detector against the block's own source.parts. D-160 records all three as ruled. Open the V1d PR after the Opus safety review." Rulings (a), (b) and (c) are RULED, not open. **The gap V18 closes:** in threshold_rules mode with `regime_detector.rules` empty or missing, every bar resolves to `default_regime` (absent = `unknown`; regime_engine.py) or to a veto's `result`; when that regime is null or missing in `strategies.regimes`, the strategy engine forecasts 0.0 on every bar and nothing says so. A hand-written config can have this shape, and the ungated twin would have it if its default were not set to the strategy's regime. **V18 (`trading-bot/tools/validate_config.py`), conditions:** (1) `regime_detector.mode` is `threshold_rules` (absent counts as it, as regime_engine.py reads it); `score` and `score_product` never read `default_regime` and are never checked; (2) `rules` empty or missing; (3) `default_regime` (absent = `unknown`) is a valid regime name, so an explicit null or an unknown name stays V7's; (4) `strategies.regimes.<default>` is null or missing; (5) ruling (a): no veto in `vetoes` has a `result` naming a non-null `strategies.regimes` entry (when one does, the bars that veto takes still trade, so V18 does not fire). **V10 de-duplication:** the top-level check is skipped when V10's case holds (`components == []` and `rules == []`), so one fault draws one line. **Ruling (c), blocks:** each `strategies.regimes.<r>.blocks[i].source` detector is checked the same way against the block's own `source.parts` (a block abstains in any regime without a `parts` entry; strategy_engine._update_block); the veto exception there is "a veto's result is a key of `parts`". **Exact messages:** top level, `VIOLATION V18 regime_detector.default_regime: '<d>' in mode 'threshold_rules' with regime_detector.rules empty or missing, so every bar resolves to '<d>' (or to a veto's result, and none holds components) -- but strategies.regimes.<d> is null or missing. This config forecasts 0.0 on every bar; point default_regime at the regime that holds the components, or move the components to strategies.regimes.<d>.`; block source, `VIOLATION V18 strategies.regimes.<r>.blocks[<i>].source.regime_detector.default_regime: '<d>' in mode 'threshold_rules' with the source detector's rules empty, so every bar resolves to '<d>' (or to a veto's result, and none is in source.parts) -- but strategies.regimes.<r>.blocks[<i>].source.parts has no '<d>' entry. This block abstains on every bar (contributes 0); point default_regime at a regime of source.parts.` `AdvancedStrategy` construction calls `validate`, so a V18 config raises `ValueError("invalid strategy_config")` before any bar is read (tested); `run_loop` and the per-variant check run `validate_config.py` too (D-158). The V18 row is in the validator table of `STRATEGY_DESIGN_GUIDE.md`. **The gate form (`strategy-research/tools/config_vehicle.py`, `gate_form_errors`):** applies only to a side finding whose `combines_as` is `regime_gate` and whose vehicle is a config. It requires (ruling 1, form; ruling (b)): `regime_detector.mode` is `threshold_rules` (absent counts as it; any other mode, `score` and `score_product` included, is refused with `<where>.regime_detector.mode=<mode>: combines_as regime_gate needs mode threshold_rules (the default): its ungated twin (D-052) empties the rules and sets default_regime, which only threshold_rules mode reads`); a non-empty `regime_detector.rules` or `regime_detector.vetoes` (else `<where>.regime_detector: combines_as regime_gate needs the gate, a non-empty regime_detector.rules or regime_detector.vetoes, and both are empty, missing or not a list; write the gate, or choose another combines_as`); and exactly one non-null `strategies.regimes` entry (else `<where>.strategies.regimes: combines_as regime_gate needs exactly one non-null regime (the regime the gate trades in), and this config has <n> <names>; set every other regime to null`). It returns messages and never raises, on any malformed input (pinned). **The ungated twin (`ungated_twin`, D-052 recipe):** a deep copy with `regime_detector.rules` set to `[]`, the `regime_detector.vetoes` key removed (not emptied: regime_engine.py and validate_config.py both read a missing key as no vetoes, and the twin has no veto at all), `regime_detector.default_regime` set to the single non-null `strategies.regimes` entry, and the detector components and everything else kept, so the twin warms up exactly like the gated config and trades the same strategy on every bar. Pure; raises ValueError with the gate-form messages when the config does not have the form. **Validated when read like the config, never stored:** after the config and the form pass, `config_vehicle_errors` runs the same checks on the twin (validate_config incl. V18, D-051/D-149) and prefixes each line `<where>.config ungated twin (D-052: rules [], vetoes dropped, default_regime '<regime>'):`; the twin is not written anywhere, and V2/V3 derive it with `ungated_twin` when they need it. Wiring: `run_phase1_research._reading_content_errors` passes the side finding's `combines_as`. **Off until V2:** the reading-time checks are reached only through a config vehicle, which `reader_proposals.CONFIG_VEHICLE_ROUTED` (False, not a flag, V2 deletes it; D-159) keeps refused; with it off a regime_gate config vehicle gets today's patch-list refusal (tested, both states). V18 in `validate_config` is live now. **The search (D-158 method): none affected, so V18 is the general check, not twin-only.** Scope 1, tracked files (author's script `v18_byte_identity.py`, `git ls-files`): 1066 files, 1060 configs; `validate()` of master and of this branch differ on 0, 0 V18 lines, 0 block regimes. The Opus safety review found that scope incomplete: `strategy-research/runs/` is gitignored and tracked runs stop at run_060. Scope 2, the operator's checkout, tracked and untracked (filesystem walk skipping `local_data`, `holdout_sealed`, `run_protocol` files, `.git` and venvs; script `untracked_v17_v18_scan.py`): 4207 files containing `regime_detector` (every `.json`, `.yaml` and `.yml` file: strategy configs, run specs `artifacts/backtest_spec.yaml` and results manifests, configs nested in them included), 5 unparseable and skipped, 3940 configs (130 distinct), 2699 of them under `strategy-research/runs/` (runs up to 097): `validate()` of master (with V17) against this branch (V17 + V18): 0 differ; and pre-V17 (b9e402c2) against master: 0 differ, so D-158's V17 also affects none of the untracked runs. **Why this cannot leak:** V18 and the form read config structure only; the twin changes the detector's routing, not any data. **Tests:** `trading-bot/tests/test_validate_config_null_default.py` (17 tests: the exact lines, absent keys, null versus missing, score modes, the veto exception both ways, V10 once, V7 untouched, block source against `parts` incl. a veto routing into `parts` and `rules` missing, `AdvancedStrategy` construction refusing a V18 config and accepting the same one pointed at the regime) and `strategy-research/tests/test_d160_regime_gate_twin.py` (22 tests: the form, the twin's exact output and purity, the twin checked and named in its failure, a vetoes-only gate with a null default and a veto into the trading regime passing the whole path (ruling a), the kill-switch shape passing with its twin default kept, malformed inputs never raising and naming what is wrong, wiring on and off). | E-077 (side-finding vehicle); Your item 3 as you traced it: _generated_protocol_holdout_block (:11680-11702) via holdout_policy.py:121-124, frozen by _freeze_run_protocol (:20076-20133), copied by variant_coin.py:287-299; the generated protocol carries {"ref": "config/campaign_data_policy.yaml#holdout_range"} instead of the dates; readers run_protocol.py:2030-2061, check_data.py:124-133, run_campaign._expected_generated_protocol (:3714/3743) resolve the ref; the console print at :16704-16706 prints the ref, never the dates; the three tests you listed (test_e061_c1_7_first_run_kit.py:360-362, test_cul339_holdout_range_from_policy.py:125, test_run_protocol_bar_equity_flag.py:109) updated; existing frozen protocols unchanged; a new test that no generated protocol contains a date inside the policy's range. Opus for the code and the safety review (sealed-window hard rule). One D-row." **What was built.** The reference has one spelling, `HOLDOUT_RANGE_REF` in `tools/holdout_policy.py`, with `holdout_ref_block()`, `is_holdout_ref()` and `resolve_holdout_block()`. The reference resolves to the policy's own [start, end], which is the literal block the generator wrote before this change; a `ref` that names anything else is refused. `_generated_protocol_holdout_block` still loads and validates the policy (a missing or malformed policy still refuses generation) and still refuses a pre-registered override that disagrees with it (an override equal to the reference now also agrees), but it returns the reference. The generator, `run_campaign._expected_generated_protocol` (same helper), `_freeze_run_protocol` (a byte copy) and `variant_coin.variant_protocol` (a deep copy) carry the reference unchanged. The holdout pause in `_route_holdout_evaluation` prints the reference, never the dates. **Readers changed.** (1) `run_protocol._protocol_holdout_block` resolves the reference against the policy, so `_resolve_holdout_window` (`--holdout`) and `_training_holdout_start` (the walk-forward bound) get exactly the values a policy-equal literal block gave them; an unknown reference is refused before any backtest. (2) `check_data.check_holdout_overlap` resolves the reference before its mismatch comparison, so the comparison runs instead of being skipped; an unknown reference prints a WARNING. (3) `run_campaign._generated_field` compares `holdout` through `_generated_holdout_field`. **Existing protocols are unchanged and still accepted.** No file is migrated. A generated protocol written before this change carries a literal block. The pre-flight treats it as equal to the reference exactly when it is dict-equal to the policy's current range, so it is accepted without change; a literal block that disagrees is still refused, as before. A promotion-only regeneration (a file rewritten anyway, because its promotion block changed) writes the expected protocol whole, so its `holdout` is the reference, whichever form the file stored (review fix 2 below). `run_protocol.py` and `check_data.py` read literal blocks exactly as before. `variant_coin.check_variant_protocol` compares a variant with its source, and both are copies of the same file, so it holds for either form. **Tests.** New `tests/test_pr9_holdout_ref_in_protocol.py`: no generated protocol (the generator's file, the expected protocol, a per-coin variant copy) holds a YYYY-MM-DD day inside the policy's holdout_range. The range is computed in-process and only compared, never printed. The same file checks that a pre-change literal file is accepted unchanged, that a disagreeing one is refused, that a regeneration writes the reference with no day inside the seal, that the pause prints the reference, and that check_data resolves it. Updated: `tests/test_e061_c1_7_first_run_kit.py`, `tests/test_cul339_holdout_range_from_policy.py`, `tests/test_run_protocol_bar_equity_flag.py`. **Review fixes (lead decisions after the Opus safety review, under the operator's rule "in-plan choices that refuse nothing existing today, change no record another stage reads, add no flag/gate/check beyond the plan").** (1) `--holdout` restores the guard a literal block gave once the policy range moved: when the protocol's block is the reference, `run_protocol._refuse_reference_protocol_windows_reaching_holdout` runs `holdout_policy.check_windows_before` (the walk-forward pre-flight's check) against the policy start and refuses before any backtest, with a message that names no date; a literal block keeps today's path exactly. (2) A promotion-only regeneration writes the reference (the stored-block override in `_generated_protocol_plan` is removed). (3) No test assertion can print a sealed date on failure: results are bound to bools or counts first (`test_pr9_holdout_ref_in_protocol.py`, `test_cul339_holdout_range_from_policy.py`). (4) `resolve_holdout_block`'s refusal names only the block's keys, not its values. (5) `config/README.md` says a generated protocol's block is the reference `HOLDOUT_RANGE_REF`, resolved by `run_protocol`, `check_data` and `run_campaign`. | CUL-339 (holdout_range has one home, the policy); run_098 defect | operator direction paste 2026-10-21, PR-9 (quoted above); Opus safety review follows | `strategy-research/tools/holdout_policy.py` (`HOLDOUT_RANGE_REF`, `holdout_ref_block`, `is_holdout_ref`, `resolve_holdout_block`); `strategy-research/workflow/run_phase1_research.py` (`_generated_protocol_holdout_block`, `_route_holdout_evaluation`); `strategy-research/tools/run_protocol.py` (`_protocol_holdout_block`, `_refuse_reference_protocol_windows_reaching_holdout`); `strategy-research/tools/check_data.py` (`check_holdout_overlap`); `strategy-research/workflow/run_campaign.py` (`_generated_field`, `_generated_holdout_field`, `_generated_protocol_plan`); `strategy-research/tests/test_pr9_holdout_ref_in_protocol.py` | built (no flag) |

Files: strategy-research/tools/analyst_queries.py (window_economics), workflow_artifacts/skills/analyst/SKILL.md :42-43, analyst/trade_efficiency.md :14-20, analyst/forecast.md :8-9, tools/analyst_session.py run_context (:104-106), docs/DATA_DICTIONARY.md section 10 (the analyst view only; the run-level cost_drag_pct definition at :288 is unchanged). One D-row. (a) window_economics, per window and total: add fees_pct_annualised (fees as percent of the starting account per 365 bars of the window), gross_per_lot_pct and fees_per_lot_pct (mean over the window's lots), turnover_annualised. cost_drag_pct is reported only when abs(gross_pct) >= 5 x fees_pct, else null with note "gross near zero: a signal finding, not a cost finding". Byte-identical for every other field; tests for the new fields and the null rule on a fixture with gross near zero. (b) Prompt order (SKILL.md :42-43, trade_efficiency.md, forecast.md): the first cost read is fees as a percent of the account (per window and per year), then gross per lot against fees per lot, then turnover; fees over the absolute gross is never the first figure; one sentence: a gross result near zero with small fees is a signal finding, and no cost claim follows from it. (c) run_context: the "Windows:" line adds the bar count per window (from the protocol or the bars file), so the lens sees "label, n bars" before its first query." **Built.** (a) `tools/window_economics.py` (the one definition behind the `window_economics` query in `analyst_queries.py` and the per-variant object stored in `profit_bars_evaluation.yaml`, so the new fields appear in both): five new cell keys after the twelve existing ones, which are unchanged in value and order (a golden test pins them): `fees_pct_annualised` = `fees_pct` x 365 / `n_bars`; `gross_per_lot_pct` = `gross_pct` / `trades` and `fees_per_lot_pct` = `fees_pct` / `trades` (the mean over the window's lots, as a percent of the window's starting account, the basis of `fees_pct`); `turnover_annualised` = `turnover` x 365 / `n_bars`; `cost_drag_note`. `cost_drag_pct` is null, with `cost_drag_note` = "gross near zero: a signal finding, not a cost finding", unless `abs(gross_pct)` >= 5 x `fees_pct`; a cell with no gross or no fees figure cannot be judged and keeps the run's recorded figure; the total uses the same formulas on its summed percents, summed trades and turnover and pooled bars. The run-level `cost_drag_pct` (`RA:build_core`, DATA_DICTIONARY section 3) and the verdict rules that read it are untouched. (b) `analyst/SKILL.md`, `analyst/trade_efficiency.md`, `analyst/forecast.md`: the cost reading order and the one sentence. (c) `tools/analyst_session.py` `run_context`: the "Windows:" line reads "label start..end, n bars" per window, n from the protocol's own windows and timeframe (days of test.start..test.end, end inclusive, times bars per day: `claim_card.window_bars`, the one definition); a window or timeframe that cannot be read keeps the line as it was. `docs/DATA_DICTIONARY.md` section 10 (the analyst view only) defines the five fields and the null rule. **Not decided here, said so:** "per 365 bars" is the operator's definition and is a year only on a daily timeframe; on a 4h or 1h run the annualised figures are per 365 bars, not per calendar year. **Review fixes (one round, lead decisions under the operator's rule on in-plan choices):** (1) the three prompt files say "per 365 bars (of the window): a year on a daily timeframe" where they said "per year"; the field definitions (and the data dictionary, which already said per 365 bars) are unchanged. (3) `_window_bars_suffix` returns "" for a step longer than a day (a weekly timeframe), so no wrong bar count is shown. The Windows line counts calendar bars of the window (end inclusive, `claim_card.window_bars`); `window_economics`' `n_bars` counts the ready bars in bars.csv; they differ where the exchange has gaps or the strategy is not ready. `gross_per_lot_pct` and `fees_per_lot_pct` are on the account basis (the window's starting account), the basis `window_economics` already reconciles; their ratio equals `gross_pct` / `fees_pct`. **Tests:** `tests/test_window_economics.py` (exact values of the new fields on the fixture windows, the byte-identity golden of the twelve existing fields, the null rule on a near-zero-gross fixture, its boundary and a negative gross, cells without figures, the total) and `tests/test_e075_pr5_analyst.py` (the Windows line with bar counts; the three prompt files carry the order and the sentence). | D-151; D-155 (window_economics) | operator direction paste 2026-10-21 (PR-5) | `strategy-research/tools/window_economics.py`; `strategy-research/tools/analyst_session.py`; `strategy-research/docs/DATA_DICTIONARY.md`; `strategy-research/workflow_artifacts/skills/analyst/SKILL.md`; `strategy-research/workflow_artifacts/skills/analyst/trade_efficiency.md`; `strategy-research/workflow_artifacts/skills/analyst/forecast.md`; `strategy-research/tests/test_window_economics.py`; `strategy-research/tests/test_e075_pr5_analyst.py` | built (no flag) |
| D-169 | 2026-10-21 | **The data gates count a window as covered only when the coin has the strategy's warm-up bars before the window start (run_098 defect: the gates ignored the warm-up). No flag.** *(PR-8 of the operator's direction paste of 2026-10-21. Numbering: D-169 was assigned by the lead; D-163..D-168 are in other unmerged PRs, so this row sits right after D-162 and the numbers between are not on this branch.)* **Direction (operator paste 2026-10-21, "PR-8", verbatim):** "PR-8 (run_098 defect: the data gate ignores the warm-up). Your item 2, second half: the gates check only the test window; the launcher's probe (launcher.py:1013-1027) then asserts readiness on the prefetch (2 x required_bars, launcher.py:950-955) and crashes uncaught. A window counts as covered only when the coin has readiness bars (component lookback plus standardise min_periods, strategy_engine.py:571-578) before the window start; otherwise the window is excluded and counted against D-042's 60 percent, with the reason in the gate's record. run_098's ADA on fold B is the test case. One PR, Sonnet, tests on the gate with a coin whose history starts inside the warm-up. One D-row." **The fault:** run_098's `asset_ada` failed with `run_protocol.py` exit 1 and no results. ADA's first confirmed bar is 2018-09-28; fold B's first window (2018-05) was already dropped; its next window (2019-01) had about 95 daily bars before it, `required_bars` was 270 (prefetch 540 = 2 x `required_bars`), and `assert probe.is_ready()` (`launcher.py:1013-1027`) failed uncaught. Both gates judged the test window only: the data-availability gate listed 2019-01 "fully available" (120 rows), and D-042's coverage (`variant_coin.window_coverage`) required only that the coin's first-bar date was on or before the window's `test.start`. **What was built:** one shared rule in `data_availability_gate.layer1_price_precheck`, the Layer-1 precheck both gates and the coverage check already call: new optional `warmup_bars` (default `None`: the structural result is returned unchanged, byte-identical). When given, a window that passes the structural checks must also have `warmup_bars` bars of the timeframe between the coin's Layer-1 first-bar date (`earliest_ohlcv_utc`, the same field the D-042 listing rule reads) and the window's start (`layer1_warmup_check`); else the precheck declines it with the reason `warm-up: <n> bars before the window start, <k> needed (...)`. (1) The data gate (`evaluate_variant`) derives `warmup_bars` from its `config` (`warmup_bars_needed`) and passes it, so the excluded window is a Layer-1 decline in the gate's `windows` record (`layer: 1`, `outcome: decline`, `missing_fraction: 1.0`, `reason: "warm-up: ..."`), never reaches the Layer-2 data touch, and rolls up as before (`refine` if another window validates, `decline` if none does); a Layer-1 decline is never a fetchable shortfall (`_gate_shortfall_fetchable`), so no data request follows. (2) D-042: `run_phase1_research._variant_coin_context` reads the run's base config (`artifacts/backtest_spec.yaml` `config`; an asset variant is an empty patch on it, G3) and wraps the precheck with `functools.partial(..., warmup_bars=N)`; `window_coverage` then lists the window under `uncovered` with the warm-up reason, `windows_run` is shorter, `fraction` falls, and `coverage_decision` applies D-042's 60 percent to the shorter list unchanged; step 2's shape check, 5a and `verify_variant_protocol`'s fresh re-check all build their context there, so they agree. No `backtest_spec.yaml`, or a config with no `regime_detector`/`strategies`: no rule, the precheck exactly as before. **The readiness number: 2 x `required_bars`, not `required_bars`.** `required_bars` is `AdvancedStrategy.required_bars` = max(regime engine `get_required_periods()`, strategy engine `get_required_periods()` = component lookback + the history ops' minimum (standardise `min_periods`), `strategy_engine.py:571-578`, 24) (`main_strategy.py:61-65`; `data_availability_gate.strategy_required_bars` computes the same from the config dict, and a test asserts equality with `AdvancedStrategy`). That is the bar at which a component's FIRST history value exists. The probe asserts `is_ready()`, which also needs each component's history to hold `max(warmup, need)` values (`strategy_engine.py:566-569`, `warmup` = `required_bars` via `set_warmup`), so readiness arrives at about component lookback + `required_bars` - 1 bars; a test feeds a 249-bar standardised component (`required_bars` 270) and finds it not ready at 270 bars and ready before 540. A rule of `required_bars` would let a coin with 270..~519 bars before the window pass the gates and still crash the probe. 2 x `required_bars` is exactly the launcher's prefetch (`launcher.py:950-955`), so a covered coin gives the probe the same input as any full-history coin and, for any config that a full-history coin passes (the launcher's 2 x `required_bars` prefetch; a regime-engine lookback above `required_bars` can need more, as the launcher's own assertion message says), the probe cannot crash on the coin's history; the cost is that a coin whose true need is a little under 2 x `required_bars` is excluded (the safe direction; the paste's wording, "component lookback plus standardise min_periods", names the first half only). Bars are counted in the protocol's timeframe: `floor((window start - first bar) / interval)`; exactly `k` bars before is covered, `k - 1` is not. **Not changed:** the Layer-2 data touch; `decide_next._fold_data_reason` (it passes no `warmup_bars`: no config exists yet when a fold is chosen, so it stays as before); a coin the Layer-1 audit holds no first-bar date for (a Kraken coin whose date was never refreshed) is not judged by the new rule, as with the existing Kraken per-coin rule. **Why this cannot leak (no lookahead):** the rule reads the strategy config (a constant) and one calendar fact, the date the coin's data begins (`earliest_ohlcv_utc`, from the venue audit or the cache's first row); it compares that date with the window's start and looks only at the span BEFORE it; no bar, return or result is read, and nothing after the window start enters the decision. **Consequences:** a window with too little history is out of coverage before any backtest runs, with the numbers in the record, instead of a crash with no results; run_098's ADA on fold B is covered or excluded window by window and D-042's 60 percent decides whether the coin runs. **Review fixes (one round):** (1) the warm-up rule is not applied at fold choice (`decide_next._fold_data_reason`, documented in its docstring), because no strategy config exists yet there, so a late-listed coin can be assigned a fold whose early windows the data gate then excludes; (2) the "cannot crash" claim above is qualified as shown; (3) `layer1_warmup_check` drops a time zone from the window start, as the structural check does for the first-bar date; (4) an unparseable timeframe and a missing first-bar date now give distinct "not judged" reasons in the record; (5) `strategy_required_bars` returns None (not judged, the rule is skipped, as for a config without `regime_detector`/`strategies`) when building the engines from a malformed config raises, instead of crashing the gate; (6) this row's own sentences. **Fact, not a decision:** a base variant whose windows are excluded for warm-up still writes a `data_requests.yaml` row (the existing Layer-1 decline path), which asks for pre-listing history nobody can fetch; whether to suppress that row is left to the operator. Tests: `test_pr8_warmup_coverage.py` (25: the number, the exclusion with reason, a full-history coin unchanged, the exact boundary, the gate record, D-042's 60 percent both sides, the wiring). *(2026-10-21 note, direction paste second set: a coin now needs twice the readiness before a window start, so the D-166 figure (a 250-bar component ready after 312 bars) describes the strategy and the gate needs 624.)* *(2026-10-21 note, PR-13: a base variant whose windows are all excluded only by the warm-up (every non-validating window a Layer-1 decline whose reason starts "warm-up:", no aux feed failing) now writes no `data_requests.yaml` row because the coin's history starts inside the warm-up and no fetch can close it, and a variant with a genuine data gap still writes its row, so the "left to the operator" fact above is settled and fold choice is unchanged; `strategy-research/workflow/run_phase1_research.py` (`_gate_excluded_only_by_warmup`, the variant-loop data gate), `strategy-research/tools/data_availability_gate.py` (`WARMUP_REASON_PREFIX`), tests in `test_pr8_warmup_coverage.py`.)* | direction paste 2026-10-21 PR-8; D-042; D-045; D-058 | operator direction paste 2026-10-21 (PR-8, quoted above); read-only diagnosis of run_098 `asset_ada` | `strategy-research/tools/data_availability_gate.py` (`strategy_required_bars`, `warmup_bars_needed`, `layer1_warmup_check`, `layer1_price_precheck`, `evaluate_variant`); `strategy-research/workflow/run_phase1_research.py` (`_variant_coin_context`); `strategy-research/tests/test_pr8_warmup_coverage.py` | built (no flag) |

(2) "PR-11 carries only the D-171 row, which cites that path and quotes the claim's substance so the versioned record stands on its own: lens, direction, horizons, n, windows claimed, vehicle decided (long-only), vehicle refused (negate, strong-long entries earn +0.022 per lot at entry >= 10), observation (bounce after forecast <= -10), half (a) untested and pre-registered for the child. No forced add." **Where the records are.** The re-run of run_098's analyst is `strategy-research/runs/run_098/artifacts/analyst_rerun/forecast.yaml` and `.../analyst_rerun/trade_efficiency.yaml`, with the query logs `.../analyst_rerun/analyst_queries/forecast.yaml` and `.../analyst_rerun/analyst_queries/trade_efficiency.yaml` and a `.../analyst_rerun/README.md` (all under `strategy-research/runs/run_098/artifacts/`). Run folders are not versioned (Option C, 2026-10-17), so those files are not in this commit; this row quotes the claim's substance and every number below is read from them. The re-run was on master e7ad5e1f, model claude-haiku-5-5, cost $1.84 (forecast) and $2.15 (trade_efficiency), each figure including a first answer the claim check refused for an invented claim key (forecast: `rationale_end`; trade_efficiency: `pass_if_note` and `fail_if_note`); the second answer of each was accepted. It is not a run and has no ledger row. The originals under `artifacts/analyst/` are untouched. **The claims (both lenses, one finding).** Setting: BTCUSD, daily bars, 730 bars in six windows (2018-05, 2019-01, 2019-09, 2020-05, 2021-01, 2021-09), one component (`anchor_position`, a Donchian position over 250 bars, standardised), 176 lots, gross -0.88 percent, fees 3.57 percent, net -4.45 percent of the account. *Forecast lens* (claim kind `conditional_behaviour`, test `short_bounce_5_20`): statement "After a close with a forecast at or below -10 (the close near the bottom of its 250-bar range, where the strategy goes short), the mean return over the next 5 and 20 bars is higher than on other bars, so the shorts entered there are covered into a bounce." Test: event `forecast <= -10`, outcome `fwd_return` at horizons 5 and 20, baseline the complement, `mean_diff`, direction greater, floor 50 events and 4 windows. Result: horizon 5, effect +0.0064, n=153 events (29 independent blocks), 4 of 4 windows with events carry the claimed sign; horizon 20, effect +0.0191, n=124 events (4 independent blocks), 4 of 4 windows. The claim says itself that horizon 20 rests on few blocks and is weaker evidence than horizon 5. Its record flags one unverified number (the "3 of 4 windows" in `pass_if`). The lot-level loss it cites: shorts -0.034 mean net return per lot (84 lots) against longs +0.024 (92 lots). *Trade-efficiency lens* (claim kind `execution_behaviour`, test `reversal`): statement "In this strategy, the bars whose forecast is -10 or lower are followed, 2 to 5 bars later, by higher returns than the other bars, in every window with such bars (4 of 4)." Same event and baseline, horizons 2, 3 and 5, direction greater, floor 100 events and 4 windows. Result: horizon 2, +0.0005, n=156; horizon 3, +0.0025, n=155; horizon 5, +0.0064, n=153; 4 of 4 windows at each. Its lot-level evidence: the 58 lots entered at a forecast of -10 or lower average -0.0219 gross per lot (31 percent positive). **The 1-bar horizon, dropped by both.** At 1 bar the pooled effect is -0.0017 (n=157), the other way, though 3 of the 4 windows point the claimed way; both lenses left it out of the claim rather than average it in. **Where the short loss sits.** Short lots by window (net per lot, lots): 2018-05 +0.0018 (33); 2019-01 -0.1854 (9); 2019-09 -0.0193 (24); 2021-09 -0.0430 (18); no short lots in 2020-05 or 2021-01. The nine lots of 2019-01 carry about 59 percent of the summed short per-lot net return (computed here from those means and counts); the other three windows with shorts add the rest, and the 2018-05 shorts break even. **Decided: the vehicle is long-only, in the config form.** The component keeps its `standardise` and then clips at zero (`clip`, `min: 0.0`), so a forecast at or below zero holds no position: the guide's "Long-only from a signed component" (`docs/STRATEGY_DESIGN_GUIDE.md`). This is the vehicle the forecast lens wrote (a config: the Donchian component, period 250, weight 1.0, `standardise` window 60 with `min_periods` 20, then `clip` min 0.0). The standardise parameters are as the analyst wrote them; the child run's builder applies D-166's window rule for a slow component (this component's lookback is 250) and D-149's standardised-forecast rule; this row does not decide them. **Refused: `negate`.** The trade-efficiency lens proposed a `negate` patch on `anchor_position` after `standardise` (a signed flip: short the strong-long forecast, long the weak one). Refused because the strong-long entries earn: the 50 lots entered at a forecast of 10 or more average +0.0221 gross per lot (40 percent positive; this is the gross mean, the figure the read measured), and a negate would trade against them; the long side as a whole is +0.0240 net against -0.0339 for the short side. **Observation, not built.** The bounce after a forecast of -10 or lower, at 5 to 20 bars (and 2 to 5 bars on the trade-efficiency read), is recorded here as an observation. Nothing is built on it: no entry on the bounce, no new component, no new test. **Half (a), untested, pre-registered.** The mirror half, "forecast >= 8 followed by a lower mean forward return than the complement at 5 bars", was not measured by either re-run read (the nearest figure, the 50-lot gross read above, is a lot-level return and not a forward-return test, and it uses 10, not 8). It is pre-registered here for the analyst of the child run: threshold 8, horizon 5 bars, direction lower, to be run as stated, with its result written whichever way it falls. **No forced add:** this row decides the vehicle and records the observation; it adds no queue entry, no flag, no gate and no code, and it reads no market data (so no ledger row). **Why this cannot leak:** every figure is a read of a finished run's own records (forward returns in those records are outcomes of the finished run, not inputs to any decision at a bar); the vehicle decided is a config of past-only rolling operations (Donchian over the past 250 bars, a trailing standardise, a clip), the same inputs as the run's own strategy. | D-149; D-165; D-166 | operator direction pastes 2026-10-21 (PR-11, second set, and the PR-11 ruling); run_098 | `strategy-research/engineering/DECISION_LOG.md` (this row only) | decided (no flag) |

| D-134 | 2026-10-15 | **Plain IC is a pickable grid criterion: `forecast_ic_median`, the median over windows of the all-bars forecast/next-bar correlation > 0.02, at least 5 windows, one fixed default (step 1a may not override it).** **What changed (no flag, no code beyond the menu entry):** one entry in `config/criterion_menu.yaml` under `criteria:` after `whole_test_sharpe_positive`: `metric: forecast_return_corr_all_bars`, `source: window`, `reducer: median`, `comparator: ">"`, `threshold: 0.02`, `card_overridable: []`, `scale_free: true`, `symbol_reducer: null`, `floor: {min_windows: 5}`. **Why the all-bars field:** `forecast_return_corr_all_bars` (`trading-bot/reporting/run_artifact.py:380-387`) is the Pearson correlation of the forecast with the next bar's return over ALL bars, no activity filter; the active-bars variant `forecast_return_corr` (`:335`) is the gated IC that the code's own comment (`:362-379`, E-039 step 5 follow-up) says is not admissible where IC over all bars is needed (the ungated-escape criterion); a regime-gated config would otherwise be judged on the bars where it chose to act. **Why 0.02 (one fixed default; the operator confirms the value at merge):** it is the same number as `residual_ic`'s threshold (`code_added_criteria`, D-039), but `residual_ic` also requires p < 0.05 and n_eff >= 30, and this entry has no significance test (it is a median-of-windows level); it sits below the skill's anchor table ceiling for crowded price-only signals (`hypothesis-design/SKILL.md` plausible_ic_upper table). Measured 2026-10-15 by the advisor over every variant window `metrics.json` in the operator's runs (bar size taken from each window's manifest timeframe; variants with at least 5 defined windows; per variant the median over its windows): HOURLY, 29 variants in 11 runs, range -0.029 to +0.018, none of the 29 above 0.02 (highest: run_070 base, 0.018). DAILY, 7 variants in 2 runs (run_065, run_094), range -0.041 to +0.058, 3 of the 7 above 0.02, all three from run_094 (UNI 0.058, ADA 0.048, BTC base 0.022): one 1-bar mean-reversion idea on one period, whose variants are correlated across coins, and which failed every profit bar (drawdown 91-97%). run_094's variants show 12 windows each because its results folders hold both attempts of the same 6 windows (the resume re-ran them), so those windows are duplicated, not 12 independent ones. Reading: on hourly bars 0.02 has not been reached by any past variant; on daily bars there is too little data to say the criterion discriminates, and the three passes come from a losing strategy (IC measures direction, not profit after costs). **Known limit** (estimates, normal approximation assuming independent windows, not measured): on daily bars (about 121 bars per window) a variant with no edge clears 0.02 roughly 1 time in 3 on 6 windows; on hourly bars (about 2,900 bars per window) about 2% on 6 windows. **Undefined windows:** a window whose correlation is undefined (None, e.g. a constant forecast) is left out of the median and does not count toward the 5-window floor (`tools/verdict_criteria_evaluator.py:1400-1403`), so a gated or sparse strategy is graded on its defined windows only. Unanimity across a hypothesis's variants tightens it. **Verified evaluator path (master `e0bc59cf`, read not changed):** `_evaluate_grid_cell_for_symbol` (`tools/verdict_criteria_evaluator.py:1355`), window branch: `_window_core_triples` (`:1256`) reads `core.<metric>` per window (`:1399`); windows whose value is not a number (None, missing key, bool) are dropped (`:1400`) and `n_windows` counts only the rest (`:1401`); `_check_floor` (`:1226`) accepts `min_windows` alone (`:1247`; `min_trades` is skipped when absent) and returns INCONCLUSIVE under it (`:1403`); `_reduce_median` (`:1138`) over the remaining values (`:1428`), then the strict comparator. A window whose correlation is undefined (e.g. a constant forecast) is therefore skipped from the median AND does not count toward the floor; with fewer than 5 defined windows the cell is INCONCLUSIVE, all-None is INCONCLUSIVE too (`:1431`), nothing crashes. The field is bar-level, not in `ZERO_TRADE_NOT_COMPUTED_FIELDS`, so a window with no trades still votes even under `zero_trade_windows_not_computed`. Step 1a's card seam `_pass_rule_from_card` (`workflow/run_phase1_research.py:9421`; override check `:9457` via `menu_criterion_overrides_violations`, `tools/verdict_criteria_evaluator.py:1783`) and the D-121 brief lint `lint_menu_shaped_pass_rule` (`:1873`) are generic over menu ids: both accept the new id with no code change and refuse any override of its fields. No test or code enumerates the live menu ids (grep of `tests/` and `workflow/`: the ids appear only as per-test picks). Note: the grid takes a RESOLVED pass_rule (an id-only rule is not menu-shaped, `_is_menu_shaped_pass_rule`); step 1a resolves it before registering, as for every entry. **DECLARED PROMPT CHANGE (no flag, to be confirmed by the operator at merge, as for D-133):** the menu is a `hypothesis_generation` required input (`workflow/run_phase1_research.py:10169-10173`), so step 1a's prompt gains a menu entry. Measured precision: that union runs only under `orchestrator.config_direct_authoring.enabled` (`:10158`; shipped default false in `config/campaign_config.yaml`, true in the operator's checkout), not with every flag off. The skill text is also changed, in a second commit of this PR: `hypothesis-design/SKILL.md` (the live-menu paragraph D-133 rewrote, ~:347-352) and `DECIDE_NEXT_CANDIDATES.md` (~:28-31) now list `forecast_ic_median` as a live entry (one clause: window, median over windows of the all-bars forecast/next-bar IC `>` 0.02, fixed threshold) and call `realized_edge_to_cost_ratio` the fourth entry; nothing else in either file changed (grep of both for other statements of the live set or its count found none). The skill is read by `hypothesis_generation` with every flag off, so that prompt changes on every path; pin updated because of the declared prompt change of D-134: `tests/test_e059_s2a_review_fixes.py::test_hypothesis_design_skill_is_byte_identical_to_master` (`_MASTER_SKILL_SHA256` 3b1f36fb... to 39d40e16...). **Tests:** 24 in `tests/test_d134_plain_ic_criterion.py` (grid PASS above, FAIL at or below the threshold, INCONCLUSIVE under 5 windows; the None-window behaviour; card gets the menu numbers and each override is refused; the 1a writer registers it; the D-121 brief lint and a flag-on brief materialization accept it). Mutation checks (measured 2026-10-15, each anchor occurring exactly once, restored): threshold 0.02 to 0.05 fails 10; comparator `>` to `>=` fails 5; `card_overridable: []` removed fails 1 (the explicit declaration test; the behaviour is unchanged because an absent key also allows nothing); `card_overridable: [threshold]` fails 6. Touched files (the new file, every test file that reads `criterion_menu`, `tests/test_doc_anchors.py`): 739 passed; `tests/test_e061_end_to_end_wiring.py` under `-m "slow or not slow"`: 33 passed; 0 failed. | E-046b S2 (the grid, card C; criterion menu) | operator decisions 2026-10-14/15 (no flag; declared prompt change); advisor measurement 2026-10-15; D-121, D-133 | `config/criterion_menu.yaml`; `tests/test_d134_plain_ic_criterion.py` | built (merge after review; operator confirms 0.02 and no flag) |

| D-141 | 2026-10-15 | **A variant whose account was ruined FAILs `whole_test_sharpe_positive`, the ruin recorded in the cell (D-101 applied to D-121's grid path).** **The ruling, verbatim (direction, operator-approved, 2026-10-15):** "option (b). A variant whose account was ruined FAILs whole_test_sharpe_positive, the ruin recorded in the cell (file, timestamp, equity). It is D-101 ("a total loss, not a crash") applied to D-121's grading path (run_phase1_research.py:7200-7206, which catches only PortfolioNotEvaluable); (a) None/INCONCLUSIVE and (c) "failed, not graded" are ruled out because both read a measured loss as unknown, D-101's stated reason. One PR from master, no flag: in the PR body list the six call sites of _whole_test_chain/load_windows with what each catches today; fix every grading path that can receive a ruined window in this PR; note why any non-grading caller is left alone. Tests: byte-identity on a run with no ruin (run_095's windows); a ruined window grades FAIL with the ruin recorded; a replay of run_096 asset_XRPUSDT. D-row cites D-101 and D-121." **Found on run_096 (fold C):** variant asset_XRPUSDT's account went below zero (equity -589.6624712066816 on 2018-09-21; the engine has no liquidation); `tools/portfolio_daily.py` raised `PortfolioRuined` (a `ValueError`, deliberately not a `PortfolioNotEvaluable`), `_with_whole_test_sharpe` (D-121) caught only `PortfolioNotEvaluable`, so the error escaped `evaluate_grid`, the grid handler recorded it and protocol_execution failed, deterministically. **What changed:** (1) `_with_whole_test_sharpe` catches `PortfolioRuined`: the summary copy gets `diagnostics.whole_test_sharpe = None` and `diagnostics.whole_test_sharpe_ruin = {file, timestamp, equity}`, one printed D-141 line; no fake number (not -inf, not -999). (2) `_evaluate_grid_cell_for_symbol`, pooled path, after the floor check: for the metric `whole_test_sharpe`, a `whole_test_sharpe_ruin` record grades `FAIL`, `value` None, `detail: {ruin: {file, timestamp, equity}}`, reason "account ruined: ... (D-101)" (the shape of residual_ic's `fully_explained` FAIL, no fake value). (3) `grid_evaluation.schema.json`: the existing `detail` key's description names `{ruin: {file, timestamp, equity}}`; no new cell key. (4) `criterion_menu.yaml` comment. No flag; with no ruin nothing new is written anywhere (byte-identical, tested). The rollup is unchanged: a FAIL cell makes the grid `refuted` (D-014), so run_096's grid now refutes on asset_XRPUSDT's cell instead of halting. **The six call sites of `_whole_test_chain` / `load_windows` (line numbers on master ec7efd26):** (1) `run_phase1_research.py:7203` `_with_whole_test_sharpe` -> `_whole_test_chain`, caught only NotEvaluable, grading (grid input): FIXED. (2) `:15153` `_portfolio_profit_metrics` (profit bars v1) -> `load_windows`, catches NotEvaluable and PortfolioRuined (D-101): left alone. (3) `:15420` `_whole_test_chain` -> `load_windows`, catches nothing by design (propagates): left alone. (4) `:15437` `_whole_test_candidate_stats` -> `_whole_test_chain`, catches nothing; callers: `_whole_test_ledger_block` (`:15441`) catches NotEvaluable and any Exception -> ledger status `error` (G10, a recorder that never raises); `_whole_test_dsr_candidate` (`:15554`) catches NotEvaluable only, but a ruined variant's ledger block is status `error` and the function returns not_evaluable at `:15604-15607` before the recompute: left alone. (5) `:15755` `_whole_test_profit_metrics` (profit bars v2) -> `_whole_test_chain`, catches NotEvaluable and PortfolioRuined (D-101, `:15758-15768`): left alone. (6) `tools/portfolio_daily.py:200` `portfolio_daily_returns` -> `load_windows`, only caller `tools/composition.py:823` (`load_block_daily_returns`) catches `ValueError` (Ruined included) into `CompositionError`; composition input, not grading: left alone. Also not a fix: `_dsr_on_current_ledger_v2` (`:8545`, holdout unlock) wraps `ValueError` into `HoldoutUnlockRefused`. Two analysis scripts outside the pipeline also call `_whole_test_chain` (`engineering/lane1_replay/fold_c_reading.py:36`, `engineering/roadmap/E-078/leave_one_out_and_bh.py:33`): one-off readers, not graded paths, left alone. **Why the DSR row needs no change:** past a ruin D-101 already leaves the Sharpe-type profit-bar rows "not computed past a ruin" while the drawdown row FAILs the bar, so the loss is never read as unknown at the bar level. **Per-symbol path:** `whole_test_sharpe_positive` has `symbol_reducer: null` and `card_overridable: []`, so it is only ever graded pooled; the per-symbol path reads `per_symbol_summary`, where the metric is never injected (unchanged, pinned in a test). **No lookahead:** no data is read that was not read before; the ruin record is the first non-positive equity row of the same files. **Not widened:** no ledger `ruined` status, no DSR change, no composition change. **Tests:** `tests/test_d141_ruined_whole_test_sharpe.py` (13): byte-identity on run_095 `base`'s six windows (Sharpe -1.0306224217274345 and the whole cell equal what master computes; the summary differs from the input only by that diagnostic), a synthetic ruined window (equity -50.0 and exactly 0.0) FAILs with the ruin in the cell and no raise, a ruined variant beside a healthy one, schema validity of the ruin cell and the whole result, the per-symbol pin, and a replay of run_096 asset_XRPUSDT (FAIL, equity -589.6624712066816, 2018-09-21). Fixtures `tests/fixtures/d141/` (equity and close columns only). **Mutation check:** (1) the new `except PortfolioRuined` replaced by another exception: 7 of 13 tests fail; (2) the evaluator branch disabled: 6 fail; (3) FAIL changed to INCONCLUSIVE: 6 fail; (4) the ruin detail dropped from the cell: 4 fail, and the ruin record not written to the diagnostics: 6 fail. A first version of the change left `ruin` unbound on the NotEvaluable branch; `tests/test_d121_brief_criteria.py` caught it and `test_a_not_evaluable_variant_is_still_inconclusive_and_never_inherits_a_ruin` now pins it. | operator ruling 2026-10-15 (direction advisory) | direction ruling 2026-10-15 (run_096 halt); D-101, D-121, D-014 | `workflow/run_phase1_research.py` (`_with_whole_test_sharpe`); `tools/verdict_criteria_evaluator.py` (`_evaluate_grid_cell_for_symbol`); `workflow_artifacts/schemas/grid_evaluation.schema.json`; `config/criterion_menu.yaml` (comment); `tests/test_d141_ruined_whole_test_sharpe.py` | built (no flag; byte-identical when no account is ruined) |
| D-144 | 2026-10-16 | **A backtest trial row written by a different protocol run replaces the earlier row in place and keeps that attempt under `superseded_attempts`; a write from the same protocol run is still skipped. Unflagged ledger-writer change; the trial count does not change.** **Found on run_096 (fold C):** a resume at protocol_execution re-runs the backtests. run_096 ran them three times (its `variants/asset_XRPUSDT/results/` holds three sets of six window directories, started 17:23Z, 17:48Z and 20:13Z on 2026-10-15), and every run of `run_protocol.py` writes a new `protocol_run_id` (`tools/run_protocol.py:165`, `:2761`). The writer's guard (H3) skipped any second `backtest` row for a `(trial_id, source)` already in the ledger, so the re-run's numbers could never reach the ledger: run_096's four rows would stay the first attempt's. All four variants hit the control D-142 deleted (86 bars per attempt, 258 over the three attempts, D-142's figure), and one of the four (`asset_XRPUSDT`, Sharpe -0.967) was a `PortfolioRuined` try. **Ruling (direction, operator-approved, 2026-10-16):** the re-run replaces the row's numbers, the earlier attempt stays under `superseded_attempts` (run id, Sharpe, `whole_test.status`, reason), the same run id is still skipped, and the trial count is unchanged (D-124: config + fold = one trial). The ruined attempt counted as a try and stays visible. **What changed (`workflow/run_phase1_research.py`):** (1) a new row carries `protocol_run_id`, copied from the summary (`_summary_protocol_run_id`); the key is absent when the summary has no id or a non-string or empty one. (2) `_record_backtest_trial` still skips when the write has no id or the same id as the row already there; a different id replaces the row at the same index, and the new row's `superseded_attempts` is the earlier list plus `_superseded_attempt(old row)` = `{protocol_run_id, sharpe, forecast_hash, whole_test_status, whole_test_reason}`, and `invalidated_artifact` / `invalidation_reason` when the old row had them (`_mark_trial_invalidated`). The new row does not inherit an invalidation: the conformance check that sets it runs again on every protocol_execution (`workflow/run_phase1_research.py:21025`, `:21060`), so a fixed re-run is a clean row and a still-violating one is re-marked. The replace does not compare config or window identity (the ruling keys on the run id alone); a changed config under the same trial_id is visible as a different `forecast_hash` in the kept attempt (found in review). (3) A row written before this row has no `protocol_run_id`: a write that carries one replaces it, and the kept attempt shows `protocol_run_id: null` (the first attempt's id was never stored and is not reconstructed). **Not changed:** `backtest_failed` rows and their guard (`_record_failed_backtest_trial`); the row count, so the DSR N and `deduplicate_trials` / `check_no_duplicate_trial_ids` keys; `campaign_memory._trial_rows` copies `forecast_hash` and `windows_sha256` only, and no reader reads `superseded_attempts`. EMPTY_VEHICLE_KINDS (tools/reader_proposals.py:143) and the no_claim shape (tools/analyst_session.py:637-640) unchanged." **What changed (`workflow_artifacts/skills/analyst/SKILL.md`, line numbers as of the last commit of PR #459):** (a) step 3, `:63-75`: a failed second query rejects that mechanism, not the behaviour; if the behaviour holds the analyst names the next mechanism and runs its query, drops the candidate only when no mechanism is left or the comparison budget says stop, names every rejected mechanism in the `no_claim` reason, and `best_rejected` is the last one.
