RUNBOOK: the third choice at `profit_bars_reached`, and a `completed_refold` row. Only existing checks: the same config on the same fold is refused as a repeat by the 5a exact-match check (`completed_no_new_hypothesis`, no backtest, no trial row; said in the help); fold D needs `orchestrator.folds`; a fold overlapping the holdout is refused by `research_folds.load_folds`. Every backtested re-run gets its trial row. Not changed: the holdout is single-use per hypothesis_id, and a re-run carries its source's. | ruling 2 item 4 and the 2026-10-13 follow-up (operator) | `tests/test_d122_refold.py` (39 tests; 19 mutations killed) | `run_campaign.py`, `run_phase1_research.py`, `tools/decide_next.py`, `tools/reader_proposals.py`, `tools/reader_findings.py`, `tools/block_registry.py`, `tools/campaign_memory.py`, `tools/verdict_criteria_evaluator.py`, `workflow_artifacts/schemas/campaign_memory.schema.json`, `docs/RUNBOOK.md` | built (operator-run command; no flag) |

| D-116 | 2026-10-13 | **The whole-test chain counts each window's first day (CUL-431's day-1 anchor defect, fixed). A declared change in output of every whole-test (profit bars v2) number; the basis tag moves to `whole_test_daily_equal_weight_v2`.** **The defect:** `chain_windows` anchored each window at its first day's close after that day's rebalance, so the fees and slippage of a day-1 fill (and, on intraday bars, the first day's P&L) were in no chained return (CUL-431; D-113 defined the "with day-1 costs" Sharpe by hand, D-114 read it). **The fix:** `chain_windows(..., start_values=...)` (`tools/portfolio_whole_test.py:416`): each window that starts the chain or follows a gap gets one more daily return, dated on its anchor day, r1 = the mean over coins of (equity at the anchor close / starting value) - 1 (`:666`), and its levels are scaled by (1 + r1); the starting value is `total_portfolio_value` on the window's first recorded bar, before that bar's rebalance (`window_start_value`, `:297`). The coverage numerator becomes the number of daily returns (each counted window day now has one) instead of returns + 1 (`:725`). A window that joins on an earlier window's day (junction / overlap) gets none, so its own day-1 fill costs stay outside the chain (the defect remains for those two kinds); since D-058 windows share no day, generated and fold protocols produce only first / gap links. `chained_buy_and_hold` measures the strategy's total return from the level before the first fill (`start_level`, `:1061`), and buy-and-hold's growth over the same day-1 span, from each first / gap window's first recorded bar to its anchor close (exactly 1.0 on daily bars, review of #406). The bar-level drawdown curve still starts at the first window's anchor close, so the first window's own day-1 drop is not in the drawdown (every later window's is). The production chain (`run_phase1_research._whole_test_chain`, `workflow/run_phase1_research.py:15136`) always passes the start values; without them `chain_windows` is exactly as before (tested). **What changes:** for every v2 grading: the Sharpe, average daily return, maximum drawdown (a gap window's day-1 drop is in it; the first point is 1 + r1), deflated Sharpe inputs (`sr_daily`, T), the buy-and-hold excess (both sides on intraday bars, the strategy side on daily bars) and the coverage figure; a day-1 return is 0 when the account at the anchor close equals its starting value (no fill, no position), and is still one more observation. **Byte-identical:** the v1 profit bars (`tools/portfolio_daily.py`, untouched), the composition, every other output. **Checked against D-114:** the pipeline's own chain now gives, on runs 089 / 084 / 088, exactly D-114's offline "with day-1" Sharpes (0.9926 / 1.4074 / 1.2953, 730 returns, 6 day-1 returns each; a test, skipped where those run folders are absent). **Basis:** `WHOLE_TEST_BASIS` is now `whole_test_daily_equal_weight_v2` (`portfolio_whole_test.py:827`), so the DSR's same-basis sample never mixes series with and without day-1 returns. Consequence, measured on the operator's ledger (`campaign_record/campaign_state.yaml`, read only): 39 trial rows carry a v1 whole-test block, 25 of them `ok`; on v2 the same-basis count K starts at 0 until those rows are recomputed (the existing append-only overlay `campaign_record/trial_sharpe_basis_recompute.yaml`, validated by `deflate_sharpe.validate_basis_overlay`; no tool builds it, and this change does not); N (all trials) is unchanged; while K is below the floor the DSR's benchmark is sigma_null (D-046). A regrade or holdout unlock of a run graded on v1 reads its basis as stale (`run_phase1_research.py:8396`, "grade again"). **Merge when no run is between its backtest and its grading:** such a run's ledger row carries a v1 block, and its grading raises (`_whole_test_dsr_candidate`, `run_phase1_research.py:15317`). | CUL-431; D-113, D-114; ruling 2 item (1) | Fable ruling 2026-10-12 (second round) item (1), confirmed by operator decision 2026-10-13 | `tools/portfolio_whole_test.py`; `workflow/run_phase1_research.py`; tests | built (no flag; declared change) |
| D-117 | 2026-10-13 | **trade_diagnostics' all-costs fields read full-precision closes (CUL-431's slippage rounding defect, fixed). A declared change in output only where rounding changed a close; byte-identical elsewhere.** **The defect:** `_all_costs_fields` (`tools/run_protocol.py:570`; CUL-414, written only under `--cost-bar-all-costs`, i.e. `orchestrator.cost_bar_all_costs`, on in the operator's checkout) measured each leg's slippage against the close in `bars.csv`, which the engine writes rounded to 6 decimals (`trading-bot/reporting/run_artifact.py:196`, `state_df.round(6)`), while fills are priced from the unrounded close; on a coin priced near 0.06 that is up to ~0.1 bps per leg (CUL-431 measured 0.107 bps on run_081). **The fix:** the window's `portfolio_states.csv` (written at full precision from the same state rows) supplies each leg's close (`_full_precision_closes`, `:621`; `_bars_with_full_precision_closes`, `:647`; used only for the all-costs fields, `:689`); a bar missing from it, or a window without the file, keeps the `bars.csv` close as before; when the file exists but misses some bars, a WARNING on stderr names the count (review of #407: never a silent fallback). Stored diagnostics are not rewritten, so a pool mixing old and new values differs by up to ~0.1 bps per leg, and a regrade is not byte-equal to the original grade. **What changes:** `gross_return_before_costs`, `slippage_paid` and `cost_paid_all` per trade, and through them the summary's `realized_edge_to_cost_ratio_all_costs` (`:1251`), the grid's all-costs `realized_edge_to_cost_ratio` and the v2 `cost_edge_ratio_min` row under the flag, for coins whose closes have more than 6 decimals. **Unchanged:** every other trade_diagnostics field, every record with the flag off (byte-identical, tested), and coins whose closes have 6 decimals or fewer (tested); stored run results are not rewritten (a regrade would read the new values). **Checked:** on run_081 (read only, the operator's checkout) every one of the 257 lots now reads `slippage_paid` = the configured slippage on both legs to 1e-6 (a test, skipped where the run folder is absent). | CUL-431; CUL-414 (D-082); ruling 2 item (1) | Fable ruling 2026-10-12 (second round) item (1), confirmed by operator decision 2026-10-13 | `tools/run_protocol.py`; `tests/test_d117_slippage_precision.py` | built (no new flag; declared change under `orchestrator.cost_bar_all_costs`) |

| D-115 | 2026-10-13 | **No validation stage: the 2024-2025 period becomes fold D, six contiguous 4-month blocks (2024-01, 2024-05, 2024-09, 2025-01, 2025-05, 2025-09), like fold A, defined in `config/folds.yaml` but outside the automatic fold order. The validation guard lets through windows that are exactly fold D's blocks, wherever it runs. Operator decision 2026-10-13 replaces ruling 2 item (0)'s lineage condition and checklist.** **Shape (advisor decision, from the code):** "folds D (2024) and E (2025)" cannot be met as written: `load_folds` requires exactly six exact 4-month tiles per fold (`tools/research_folds.py:157`), and fold confirmation needs at least 4 windows (`tools/fold_confirm.py:109`, `MIN_WINDOWS = 4`); a single year holds only three tiles. So one fold D of six blocks over 2024-2025. **Decide-next unchanged (D-085):** `FOLD_ORDER` stays A, B, C (`research_folds.py:65`) and `folds.yaml`'s `order` stays [A, B, C]; `assign_fold` (`:282`) and `folds_used_by` (`:276`) read only that order, so a lineage that used A, B and C is still `folds_exhausted`, never fold D, and a 2024 window still counts for no fold. Fold D is reached only through the operator's re-run command (a later PR). **What changes (code):** `FOLD_D` and `ALL_FOLDS` = A, B, C, D (`:68`, `:70`); `load_folds` now requires the folds file to hold exactly the keys A, B, C, D (`:177`, the one changed check), checks A-C against the research period as before and D against the 2024-2025 period (`:203`) and the holdout (`:208`); `check_fold_id` accepts D (`:404`), so a fold-D run's memory entry and trial rows record it. The guard `assert_windows_clear_of_validation` (`:331`) still refuses any window overlapping 2024-2025 unless its label, start and end are exactly one of fold D's blocks; fold D's windows come from `load_fold_d_windows` (`:363`) and are read only when some window overlaps 2024-2025. Every place the guard runs passes them: the generator (`workflow/run_phase1_research.py:11469`, the loader at `:11483`), the launch pre-flight for generated protocols and for pinned or run_context / last_escalation-resolved files (both through that same function), and `tools/run_protocol.py:2078` (the choke point every backtest passes). So any protocol, generated or not, whose 2024-2025 windows are exactly fold D's blocks passes the guard. **A brief carrying `fold: "D"`** is generated only under `orchestrator.folds.enabled` (`run_phase1_research.py:11516`, in `_protocol_windows_from_constraints`, which now receives the flag, `:11493`; pre-flight `workflow/run_campaign.py:3705`); flag off it is refused with master's exact message ("fold 'D' is not one of ['A', 'B', 'C']"), before anything else is read, and generation for every other brief reads the flag exactly as on master. Nothing reads its per-symbol meaning ("already used in a historical walk-forward"). **What the exact-match repeat check says for a legacy-family idea on 2024: never REPEAT.** The check reads only campaign memory (`tools/novelty.py:218-230`, `_tested_variants`); the memory in the operator's checkout starts at run_065, so no earlier run has an entry, and an entry marked `legacy: true` is skipped (`:225`). | D-113 | 2026-10-12 | **The whole-test chain's coverage denominator no longer counts planned protocol gap days. Counted days = the days inside some window's nominal span (the union of the protocol's test.start..test.end, end inclusive), minus the engine tail/head days no window's effective span covers (unchanged). Today the denominator is the whole nominal span from the first window's start to the last window's end, and "Protocol gap days stay counted (G2)" (`tools/portfolio_whole_test.py:481-485`, code `:656-667`); the floor is unchanged, `WHOLE_TEST_MIN_COVERAGE` = `portfolio_daily.PORTFOLIO_MIN_COMMON_DAY_COVERAGE` = 0.9 (`:97-100`, `tools/portfolio_daily.py:38`).** **Why:** fold B's six blocks are interleaved with planned gaps (D-108), so the chain spans 1,341 calendar days of which 611 are protocol gap days that no backtest was asked to trade; counting them made the whole-test Sharpe, D-108's first input, NOT_EVALUABLE on all three fold-B runs (724 daily returns + 1 = 54.1% of 1,341 < 0.9). A gap the protocol plans is not missing data. **What stays:** a day missing INSIDE a window still counts in the denominator and costs two daily returns in the numerator (as today), so the floor still catches missing data; the per-window floor (`portfolio_daily.window_common_curve`) is untouched; the numerator stays (daily returns + 1): the +1 is the first window's anchor day, and each later window's first day has no return either, contiguous or not (`chain_windows` counts every such link in `n_gap_links`, fold A's contiguous junctions included) -- on fold B 6 of the 730 days inside the windows have no return, 5 beyond the +1; conservative, recorded, not changed. **No flag.** A declared change in output for protocols with gaps (`n_gap_days` > 0): the coverage figure and `n_counted_calendar_days` (`n_calendar_days` stays the full nominal span) and whatever reads them; byte-identical for contiguous protocols (fold A, where the union of the nominal spans is the whole span). The fix PR lists every reader of the chain's coverage and counted days before coding, and lists, read-only, the past runs with `n_gap_days` > 0 (their stored results do not change; a regrade would), and says whether the ledger's whole-test basis tag (`WHOLE_TEST_BASIS`, `tools/portfolio_whole_test.py:746-749`, which the DSR's same-basis sample matches exactly) must change so values under the two denominators are not pooled. **D-108's Sharpe input, fixed here before any computation:** the existing `whole_test_sharpe` over the chain's existing `daily_returns` (no new Sharpe), reported twice: (a) without day-1 costs = exactly those returns (the chain anchors each window at its first day's close after that day's rebalance, CUL-431); (b) with day-1 costs = the same returns plus one return per window, dated on its anchor day (the first window's included; that date carries no return in (a)): the equal-weight mean over coins of `postRebalance_total_value` / `total_portfolio_value` on the anchor day's bar, minus 1 -- `total_portfolio_value` is the mark before that bar's rebalance (on the window's first bar, its starting cash, e.g. 1000.0 in `runs/run_084`) and `postRebalance_total_value` the value after it, so the return is that bar's fees and slippage, 0 when it makes no trade. Defined for one bar per day (lane 1's daily runs); with more bars on the anchor day the computation fails loudly instead. The coverage figure reported is the chain's own, the same for (a) and (b). D-108's rule is then read on both; if they give different 2-of-3 outcomes, that is reported plainly and goes to Fable before anything is chosen. **Declared: this fix is made after partial fold-B results were seen.** Seen before this row: the whole-test Sharpe was NOT_EVALUABLE on runs 089 / 084 / 088 (the coverage figures above); the log's `median_sharpe` (the mean of the per-coin medians of per-window Sharpes, `workflow/run_campaign.py:2670-2672`) 0.902 / -2.793 / -3.501 (per coin BTC / ETH / LTC: 1.999 / -0.797 / 1.505; -0.264 / -7.968 / -0.147; -3.501 / null / null); the grid labels and idea statuses validated (run_089, `realized_edge_to_cost_ratio` 39.92 > 2.2, 110 trades) / refuted (run_084, era signs disagree, ratio INCONCLUSIVE at 70 trades) / refuted (run_088, the same at 42 trades), and run_089's automatic registry entry; lots 110 / 70 / 42 (window-end 14 / 19 / 18), signal exits 65 / 32 / 17; the base run's curve-based gross/cost 62.3 (D-108's second input). No whole-test Sharpe of any kind (with or without day-1 costs, any coverage rule) was computed on fold B before this row. The coverage rule was chosen for the protocol shape (planned gaps are not data), not for a result: it is the simplest reading under which an interleaved fold can be graded on the existing chain, and the same rule would apply had the per-coin medians been all positive or all negative. | lane 1 (D-108); E-062 G2 (D-034..D-036); CUL-431 | Fable ruling 2026-10-12 (operator's point 1), operator agreed | `tools/portfolio_whole_test.py` (fix PR follows this row) | decided (docs only; fix PR next) |

the "test was run" and evidence checks stay on the exact hash; (4) the "never run" refusal names the queries that ran the same test under another floor and says to re-run with the `floor` parameter (the retry can act on it); | D-093 | 2026-10-09 | **Post-merge review of #359 (E-075 PR-5, D-092): the analyst's answer check closes three holes, a malformed answer is refused instead of crashing the stage, and two flag interactions are fixed (all under `orchestrator.analyst.enabled`, off by default).** An independent read-only review (2026-10-09) found: (1) **MEDIUM, a best-of-N score.** `in_run_score` kept the HIGHEST window agreement over every run of the claim test, and a test's `spec_hash` does not include the variant, so re-running the test on other graded variants could only raise the score (reproduced: 2 of 5 windows on base, score 0; plus 5 of 5 on another variant, score 3). Now the LOWEST over every run of the test. (2) **MEDIUM, unchecked numbers** *(the refusal of an unverified prose number: superseded by D-096, 2026-10-10; its detection stays)*. Only `evidence` was checked against the query log; the claim's free text (`statement`, `pass_if`, `fail_if`, `rationale`, which becomes the reading's `explanation`) could quote numbers no query returned, and `evidence` could cite an unrelated call (reproduced: a rationale "9.9%" with `evidence: [q2:variant=base]`, a `list_columns` call, accepted). Now `evidence` must cite the claim test's own `conditional_effect` result (a path under `horizons.`), and every number standing on its own in the four text fields and in the no-claim `reason` and `best_rejected.statement` (`analyst_session.prose_number_errors`) must be: 0 or 100%; a horizon of a cited `horizons.<h>.` path or a number of the claim's own test blocks, written exactly (no %, no exponent); or a cited value rounded to every digit it shows (`x%` is x/100 or x; a written sign must match the cited sign, an unsigned number is compared without sign). `.5`, `1,234`, `1.2e-3` and `24h` count as numbers; one glued after a word character (`past_return_24`, `run_074`) is a name. (3) **MEDIUM, a crash.** `why_query: [q2]`, `killed_by: [q1]`, an `outcome` that is a list, or `evidence` that is a number raised `TypeError` inside `check_answer`, which escaped the stage before the retry, and a claim the claim card cannot hash (a 400-digit integer: `OverflowError` in `claim_tests._canon`) did the same: each is now an ordinary refusal (a parametrized test feeds lists, mappings, numbers and nulls into the six claim-answer keys and `killed_by`). (4) **LOW, `why_query` checked in name only:** any successful call (`list_columns`, `describe`) satisfied it; it must now be a `conditional_effect`, `trade_slice` or `event_study` call, and a `conditional_effect` on another selector than the claim's test. (5) **LOW:** the query logs and session records (`artifacts/analyst_queries/`, `artifacts/analyst/`) are now cleared with the stage's other per-attempt directories, so a re-attempt starts a new log and budget (they exist only under the flag). Fixed: fold rows are read with `explore_confirm.fold_rows`, one claim per finding id, replacing the E-072 row and the memory claim with the same id; the tests now write the ledger through the real writer. **Rule for several rows of one claim (operator, 2026-10-08):** `confirmed` if any fold confirmed it, else `not_confirmed` if any fold refuted it, else `not_measurable`, never decided by key order; a refutation on another fold stays recorded in `folds` (`@run_100` sorts before `@run_99` as text); `fold_confirmed` lists the confirming folds, `confirmed` becomes a list with the numbers of those rows only, and a new `folds` field lists every measured fold with its status (no number). **Every statement is masked**, a confirmed claim's too: it was written on the run that inspired it, so its numbers are exploratory; the confirmed numbers are in `confirmed`. (2) **MEDIUM, a free calendar effect.** `describe` / `distribution` of `past_return_<n>` or `trailing_vol_<n>` grouped by hour or weekday counted 0 comparisons, yet `past_return_1` at bar t+1 is `fwd_return` h=1 at bar t (the reviewer reproduced `conditional_effect`'s calendar number exactly). Review round 1 of this PR showed the same through a price level (`describe("close", by="hour")`: two bucket means differ by the mean move between the buckets). Now EVERY `describe` / `distribution` grouped by hour, weekday or regime (a persistent regime label is nearly a regime selector) counts one comparison per group, whatever the column, charged before any number is computed; no grouping, or a grouping by window or coin, stays free. (3) **LOW:** a NaN or infinite parameter (JSON accepts them) ran but was logged and returned as `None`, so the test block was not the one that ran: refused and logged. (4) **LOW:** a negative `n_comparisons` in a hand-edited log gave budget back: clamped at 0 (a deleted log still resets the budget; PR-5 binds one log per session). (5) **LOW:** `event_study` counted a checkpoint with a missing close in `n` and returned no mean: only finite values count. (6) **Review round 1 (LOW):** a fold row without a statement or kind (built when the source proposal was unreadable) no longer blanks the claim (the first row that has one, else the claim it replaces); a `kind` outside the closed claim kinds is shown as none (a refused claim's kind is free text); test names are not shown (free text); digits glued to a word (`h24`, `5of6`, `Sharpe1.2`) are masked in the view; a fold status outside the vocabulary is `not_measurable` in `folds` too; a numpy NaN parameter is refused like a Python one. **Accepted by the operator (2026-10-08):** E-072 files a merged duplicate under its primary id (`merged_finding_ids`); a child built from the duplicate id writes a fold row under the duplicate, so the view may show two claims (the primary pending, the duplicate measured). | D-089 | 2026-10-08 | **Two operator decisions on claim confirmation, under the existing `orchestrator.folds.enabled` flag (off by default; no new flag): (a) a claim of kind `execution_behaviour` may change nothing (`vehicle: []`); (b) several coins are pooled per window before the noise rule (option C).** **(a) Claims that change nothing.** An `execution_behaviour` claim is about today's strategy as it is: its vehicle may be empty, the child re-runs the same strategy on the next fold its lineage has not used, and the strategy's own behaviour there is the test. Every other claim kind still needs a non-empty vehicle. Code: `reader_proposals._check_envelope` accepts `vehicle: []` only when the claim's kind is in `EMPTY_VEHICLE_KINDS` (= `claim_card.FOLDS_CLAIM_KINDS`, tested equal) and refuses it otherwise with its own message; a missing vehicle is still refused under the flag (its message now names the empty form). Downstream nothing new: `flatten_reading` hands `config_change: []` to decide-next, whose `side_finding_start` already starts from the source run's config unchanged (novelty `NOT_APPLICABLE`, "a new claim on it"), and `fold_confirm.confirm_on_fold` skips the vehicle-in-config check for an empty vehicle. **Gap found and closed here, needed by (a):** `reader_findings.side_finding_review` called `check_claim(claim)` without `folds=True`, so an `execution_behaviour` claim was refused at the reading's content check and again at decide-next (`next_test_refused`) even under the flag; it now takes `folds=` and its three callers pass it only under the flag (`run_phase1_research._reading_content_errors` and `_review_written_reading` via `_claim_check_folds()`, `decide_next._side_finding_review` when `inputs["folds"]` is set). **(b) Several coins, option C.** In each window the coins are pooled into ONE value first (the test's statistic over every coin's events of that window, exactly as `claim_measure._per_coin` pools one coin's windows), then "all but one window" is applied over the fold's windows (at least 4 with a value). So a 3-coin claim faces the same ~11% chance per fold (6 windows, symmetric null) as a one-coin claim; before, each coin x window cell counted, so 3 coins needed 17 of 18 cells. Code: `fold_confirm.measure_on_fold` is the new default measurement: it is `explore_confirm.measure_on_windows` unchanged, plus, when the windows hold several coins, `per_window_coins_pooled` on each measured horizon (`fold_confirm.pooled_per_window`, generic over bar and trade windows); `grade_test` counts those windows; a several-coin measurement without them is `not_measurable` (never graded per cell); the row records `window_basis: coins_pooled_per_window`. **One coin is measured exactly as before** (the row equals the pre-D-089 row field for field, tested at 6, 5 and 4 of 6). Tests (`tests/test_d089_vehicle_and_coins.py`): an empty vehicle accepted for `execution_behaviour` and refused for three other kinds; a claim with an empty vehicle confirmed on the unchanged strategy; 3 coins x 6 windows: 5 of 6 pooled windows confirm, 4 of 6 do not; a window where one coin of three plants the reverse is pooled, not voted; the pooled value equals a hand computation; flag off, every call is as before. One existing assertion changed deliberately (`test_e077_pr2_confirm.py::test_each_envelope_key_is_checked`: an empty vehicle is now refused for `event_behaviour`, not for `execution_behaviour`). **Review round 1 of this PR (fixed):** (i) an empty vehicle is measured only on today's strategy: `confirm_on_fold` compares the measured variant's `strategy_config.json` with the brief's `candidate.start_config` by value (`nearest_build.config_diff`, CUL-412's own definition of "1b changed it"; round 2 replaced a hash comparison, which refused a config 1b only retyped, 1.0 as 1) and is `not_measurable` (`strategy_changed_since_the_claim`, naming the changed leaves) when they differ or either is missing; an empty vehicle on a source with no block config (a composition) is INFEASIBLE at decide-next (`empty_vehicle_needs_a_source_config`), since its child could never be measured; (ii) the remaining claim checks without `folds` (`reader_findings._merge_key` / `side_finding_merges`, the D-083 `reading_structure`) take it under the flag, so two identical `execution_behaviour` findings merge; (iii) **accepted by the operator (2026-10-08), not changed:** a finding with no config change keeps novelty `NOT_APPLICABLE`, so two different `execution_behaviour` claims of one source may each re-run the same config on the same fold (a deterministic re-run: no window shopping, only compute). Keying it like a config (tried in round 1, reverted in round 2) would make every later empty-vehicle claim of that source a REPEAT for good, since decide-next always gives that source's lineage the same next fold; (iv) the coins come from the windows' symbols, a label without "/" names no coin (graded as before), and a pooling failure is recorded on its test only. Accepted and noted, not changed: with option C a window's complement baseline pools every coin's bars, so a pooled sign can come partly from events concentrating on the coin with the higher drift (a Simpson-type composition, inherent to pooling). Mutation check: 23 of 23 planted errors killed (the reverted novelty change's own check dropped). **Flag off:** an envelope (and so a vehicle) is accepted from a model only under the flag; a FILE carrying `vehicle: []` with kind `execution_behaviour` now loads (it was refused), but such a file is only written under the flag. **Not done here:** the trade-level tests are still not measured on a fold (`measure_on_windows` calls `measure_test` without `trade_tests`, and `fold_confirm` / `side_finding_review` check claims without it), so a trade-level claim is `not_measurable` there today: needed by the trade-efficiency analyst, proposed for E-075 PR-5. | D-088 | 2026-10-08 | **The analyst's query engine and memory view: six fixed functions over one saved run, and the list of earlier claims with numbers for confirmed ones only (E-075 PR-4, CUL-422; plan A1.11 items 5 and 7). A pure module, no flag.** (1) `tools/analyst_queries.py`: `list_columns()`, `describe(column, by)`, `distribution(column, by, bins)`, `conditional_effect(condition, horizons, outcome, baseline, statistic, direction, by)`, `trade_slice(filter, agg, by)`, `event_study(trade_filter, bars_before, bars_after)`, each with `variant="base"`; over ONE run directory passed in (its `bars.csv`, `trades.json`, `trade_diagnostics.json` named by the variant's `protocol_result.yaml`, every resolved path refused unless inside the run; a run directory under `local_data` or `holdout_sealed` refused; `claim_tests.load_variant_bars` gets no cache, so no warm-up file and nothing under `trading-bot/local_data/` is opened; any bar at or after the holdout start read from the data policy refused, an unreadable policy refuses everything). No placebo arm, no outcome layer, no exploration/confirmation split (judgement section 4: dropped under D4 plus folds). (2) `conditional_effect` takes a claim-engine selector (the six bar selectors, the gated `trailing_vol` field, or the PR-3 `trade` selector) and calls `claim_tests.effect_sizes` on the `TestSpec` built from the slots, so its numbers are the ones `claim_measure.measure_test` reads (tested for every selector x outcome x baseline x statistic the bar slots allow and for the trade family, and the returned `test` block is measured by `claim_measure` to the same numbers); its result carries that block, its `spec_hash` and whether `claim_card` accepts it today (`trailing_vol` is not accepted by a claim card yet). (3) Comparisons: one returned number that relates a condition or group to an outcome; `conditional_effect` = horizons x groups (1 group without `by`, else the windows / coins returned; the pooled number beside a `by` is not recounted), `trade_slice` = groups x its `trade_net_return` / `post_exit_return` aggregates that are not a `count`, `event_study` = the "after" checkpoints, the rest 0; a session may spend 150, a call that would pass it is refused ("comparison budget spent: write your proposal"). Caps per call: 12 groups, 6 horizons, 4,000 characters of result; over any cap the call is refused and counts 0. (4) The log `artifacts/analyst_queries.yaml`: every call, refused ones included, is appended (atomic rewrite, fsync) BEFORE its result is returned, with a sequential id `q<n>`, the parameters, status, reason, comparisons and the cumulative total, a short digest, the result itself (at most 4,000 characters, so PR-5 can resolve citations against it) and its sha256; an unexpected error is logged as `error` and re-raised; ids and the running total continue across engines on the same file. (5) Features at bar t only: conditions are the claim engine's; `describe` / `distribution` read only columns the data dictionary marks `close` or `fill` (plus `past_return_<n>` / `trailing_vol_<n>`); `exit` / `after` are outcome-only, `meta` a label, `end` / `run` never (finding A1: the balances); a column absent from the dictionary is refused (the dictionary's `<reserved feed>` row, a bare placeholder, matches nothing); event_study's "after" half is labelled an outcome and its "before" half cannot change with later bars (tested). (6) `tools/analyst_memory_view.py`: per earlier claim `claim_id, source_run, statement, kind, fold_observed, fold_confirmed, status` with status confirmed / not_confirmed / not_measurable / pending, and `confirmed: {fold, effect, agreement}` for CONFIRMED claims only; a run's own claim is `pending` (`not_measurable` if it carries no test), a ledger row replaces it; no exploratory effect, no window count, no reason text (reasons quote "windows 3 of 6"), and the statement of an unconfirmed claim has its number literals masked with `explore_confirm.mask_numbers` (run_074's statement quotes "forecast_return_corr=-0.0057"), its mechanical test spec left readable. | D-084 | 2026-10-08 | **A window with no trades does not vote in the grid's window criteria, behind one flag, `orchestrator.zero_trade_windows_not_computed.enabled` (CUL-415, audit finding A8; off by default; a declared output change when on).** The engine writes 0.0, not null, for the trade-based `core` fields of a window without trades (`net_return_pct`, `max_drawdown_pct`, `win_rate`, `sharpe` when undefined, ...). Flag off that 0.0 is a value in the grid: it votes in the `sign_consistent_by_era` era median (and can make an era median exactly 0, a FAIL), in median / mean / min / max / fraction_above (drawdown 0.0 flatters, win_rate 0.0 penalises), and counts toward `floor.min_windows`. Flag on, in a window-source criterion whose metric is computed from the window's trades (`verdict_criteria_evaluator.ZERO_TRADE_NOT_COMPUTED_FIELDS`), a window with `core.trade_count == 0` is not computed: it neither votes nor counts toward `n_windows` / `floor.min_windows`, and the cell records it in `skipped_windows` (count, windows, reason); too few windows left is INCONCLUSIVE, never FAIL. Skipping never removes a group from the vote (review fix, PR #349): an era represented before the skip with no window left after it is recorded in `skipped_windows.eras_left_empty` and makes a `sign_consistent_by_era` cell INCONCLUSIVE ("era <e>: every window had no trades -- the sign cannot be judged there"), never PASS; a FAIL the remaining eras already decide stays FAIL. So losing an era can turn FAIL into INCONCLUSIVE, never into PASS, and a flag-on PASS always rests on every era the flag-off cell compared -- which is why the flag needs no dependency on `profit_bars_v2`'s single-era rule. A symbol whose windows are all skipped keeps its own `per_symbol_all` cell (INCONCLUSIVE, never PASS). A FAIL caused only by a zero-trade 0.0 inside an era (or a scalar reducer) that keeps a trading window can still become PASS: that is the change the flag makes. A null or missing `trade_count` is unknown, never zero (kept). Bar-level fields (`trade_count`, the forecast/return correlations, `sigma_bar_bps`) and pooled criteria are unchanged. Handled in the strategy-research reducer layer, reading `trade_count`: the engine's `metrics.json` is unchanged (no new field; `run_protocol`'s progress print formats `max_drawdown_pct` with `:.1f` and would crash on a null). Not changed, left for the operator: `run_protocol`'s per-symbol summaries (`median_win_rate`, `median_gross_pnl`, `max_abs_drawdown_pct`, which the v1 profit bar reads) still include a zero-trade window's 0.0; they are not on the grid's path. Requires `grid_evaluation`. Flag off byte-identical (tested); runs 070-074 have no zero-trade window and grade identically flag on and off. | D-083 | 2026-10-08 | **Reader citations are checked against their values, and two readers proposing the same test give one finding (E-073 step 2, P-CUL-81), behind the epic's one flag `orchestrator.observable_backtest`.** (1) Value rule (`reader_proposals.check_citation_values`, `CITATION_VALUE_RULE`): a citation is a field path from a file the reader received (a known root, a top-level key, or the file's own name) followed by `=` or `:` and a value; the path must exist; a number matches when it is the file's value rounded to the digits the reader wrote (within half a unit of its last digit; a trailing `%` also matches the value x 100); text, booleans and null match exactly; with `[*]` any element may match; a path to a mapping or list, or with no value, is not value-checked. Compared with the files the reader actually received: under E-072 the exploration copies, never the all-window files. Prose is not checked. (2) A missing or mis-valued citation gets the reader's ONE retry (the existing one: a shape retry spends it), with a code-written list (path, cited value, problem -- never a value from the files) and the reader's own answer verbatim; still wrong, the reading is kept and the citation recorded in `artifacts/citation_checks/<category>.yaml`, and decide-next shows a `citation_mismatch` warning on that side finding (never a reason, never a rank change). A refused retry answer keeps the first answer; a spent token budget skips the retry; a check error is recorded. Never a stop. (3) In-run dedup (`reader_findings.side_finding_merges`): side findings with the same set of claim-test spec_hashes and the same `config_change` are one finding (`artifacts/side_finding_merges.yaml`, primary = first in category order): one E-072 confirmation record and one look, listing every source (`sources`, `merged_finding_ids`); one decide-next candidate with the PRIMARY's scores (`merged_sources`), not the higher of the two as the existing collapse keeps; a run built from a duplicate resolves the primary's pending record. Readers share their inputs, so a merge is never agreement or extra evidence (D-079). (4) Off: byte-identical (tested). Defaults taken for the operator to confirm: kept-but-flagged (alternative: drop the side finding), a missing path retried like a wrong value, the half-unit rounding tolerance (alternative: a full unit, which also accepts truncation), the primary's scores for a merged candidate, exact spec_hash-set equality (alternative: any shared test). Measured on run_073's real readings (offline, no model call): 55 citations with a path, 30 match, 0 mis-valued, 21 missing (e.g. `grid_evaluation.residual_ic...` for `grid.residual_ic...`), so each of the 3 readings that ran would have used its retry; 0 duplicate side findings. Also fixed: `run_campaign` logged every candidate warning as an unknown class name (`w['name']`), a KeyError on a side finding's warnings; now one line per kind (`_candidate_warning_lines`). Final design (after two reviews of PR #346; simplified, no per-case rules): the retry note lists the cited path, the cited value and the problem ("value does not match the file", or "path not found" with up to 3 real paths that end in the same key, rooted at the file stem so each is citable as written) and never a value from the files, so the retry must re-read its inputs; the retry answer is kept only with strictly fewer bad citations AND the same side findings (count, item kind, claim kind, spec_hash set: `reader_findings.reading_structure`), else the first answer (`first_attempt_bad`, `retry_attempt_bad`, `kept_answer` recorded); one number rule (the file's value rounded to the decimals written, half a unit of the last digit; `%` also x 100) with one exception (a `0` written with fewer than 2 decimals matches only |x| < 0.005); a number written any other way (`3k`, `approx 120`, `~0.08`, a word) is not checked (`path_only`), never a mismatch; text matches when the file's whole text, ignoring case and surrounding spaces, is the cited value (more words may follow), so long free text is not a citation target; keys may hold inner hyphens (`per_window.2022-09`), a hyphenated key the file does not have is cut at its first hyphen; quoted bracket keys; dictionary-style roots (`summary.`, `trades[]`, `slices.`) are not checked; a path resolves in the file it names, else the first received file that has it (own report first); the merge key includes `claim.kind`. Re-measured on run_073 (offline): 55 citations with a path, 30 match, 0 mis-valued, 21 missing, 4 not checked (2 path_only, 2 not_a_value); 20 of the 21 missing carry suggestions (45), all 45 re-resolve as written, and in 18 the first suggestion holds the cited value. | plan item 2 (E-073) | `engineering/delivery_plan_readers.md` (D-079) | `tools/reader_proposals.py` (`check_citation_values`, `value_matches`, `CITATION_VALUE_RULE`, `CITATION_CHECKS_DIR`); `tools/reader_findings.py` (`side_finding_merges`, `merges_doc`, `merge_index`, `MERGE_RULE`); `tools/explore_confirm.py` (`confirm_findings(merges=)`, `pending_for`); `tools/decide_next.py` (`_observable_inputs`, `_fold_in_run_merges`, `CITATION_WARNING`); `workflow/run_phase1_research.py` (`_reader_received_files`, `_citation_value_check`, `_citation_retry_note`, `_write_citation_check`, `run_reader_worker`, `_record_side_finding_merges`, `DATA_DICTIONARY_LINE`); `workflow/run_campaign.py` (`_candidate_warning_lines`); `workflow_artifacts/schemas/decision_record.schema.json`; `config/campaign_config.yaml`; `config/feature_flag_register.yaml`; `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`; `tests/test_e073_citations_dedup.py`. | D-082 | 2026-10-08 | **The D-038 "survives 2x costs" bar counts fees AND slippage, behind one flag, `orchestrator.cost_bar_all_costs.enabled` (CUL-414, audit finding A7; off by default; a declared output change when on).** Flag off the ratio divides `realized_return` (fill to fill, so already net of the modelled slippage) by `cost_paid` (the configured fee x 2, no slippage): with the Kraken perp costs of runs 070-074 it passes at about 27 bps of gross edge per trade on BTC and 37 on SOL/UNI. Flag on, both places of the bar compare the gross edge per trade BEFORE fees and slippage with fees + slippage, both legs, as the engine modelled them for that trade: about 33 bps on BTC and 55 on SOL/UNI at > 2.2. (1) `run_protocol.py --cost-bar-all-costs` (passed by `run_phase1_research` only under the flag) appends three fields to every trade record -- `gross_return_before_costs` (the return at the bar closes the two fills were priced from; `MockExecutionHandler` fills at close x (1 +/- slippage)), `slippage_paid` (both legs, fill vs that close) and `cost_paid_all` (= `cost_paid` + `slippage_paid`) -- and `realized_edge_to_cost_ratio_all_costs` to the summary; computed from what is already recorded (fills, bars.csv closes, the fee), no engine change. (2) The menu criterion `realized_edge_to_cost_ratio` reads that field (`evaluate_grid(cost_bar_all_costs=True)`); the legacy pass rule (`evaluate_pass_rule_criteria(cost_bar_all_costs=True)`, `pass_rule_evaluation.yaml`, written unless the legacy label is retired) uses the same basis, by the same remap, so the two artifacts never disagree on it in one run; profit_bars_v2's `cost_edge_ratio_min` uses `pooled_edge_to_cost_ratio(all_costs=True)` and records `detail.cost_basis: fees_and_slippage`. A record or artifact without the new fields is NOT_EVALUABLE / INCONCLUSIVE, never the fee-only ratio; if any one trade lacks them, neither bar computes the ratio on the others (the summary records the count, `realized_edge_to_cost_ratio_all_costs_missing_trades`). (3) A holdout spend refuses `bars_changed` when the variant's cost row was graded on another cost basis than the flag gives now. (4) Existing fields keep their values (`cost_paid` stays the fee: `_v2_charged_fees` checks it); flag off byte-identical (tested). The threshold 2.2 is unchanged; its basis text (2 + slippage/fee) assumed a fee-only denominator and is for the operator to restate. | D-046 | 2026-09-29 | **DSR formula and small-sample rule (E-062 S2b-2, S2B2_FINDINGS G3/G4).** (1) The DSR becomes the exact Bailey & Lopez de Prado 2014 form on the whole-test daily basis: DSR = Phi((SR - SR0) * sqrt(T-1) / sqrt(1 - g3*SR + ((g4-1)/4)*SR^2)), SR daily and not annualised, g3 skew, g4 raw kurtosis of the candidate's daily portfolio returns, Z(N) = (1-gamma)*Phi^-1(1-1/N) + gamma*Phi^-1(1-1/(e*N)), N = every counted trial (never shrinks). (2) With K same-basis trial Sharpes: if K >= `dsr_min_same_basis_trials` (10, a signed key in profitability_bars.yaml), SR0 = max(mu_K, 0) + max(sigma_K, sigma_null) * Z(N); if K < 10, SR0 = sigma_null * Z(N) (pure-luck benchmark), so the bar is always computable and a variant never needs a rerun to be graded. sigma_null = 1/sqrt(T-1), the null (true SR = 0) standard error, where the skew/kurtosis terms vanish. Measured hurdle (normal returns, DSR > 0.95): N=12 needs annualised Sharpe 1.22 at T=2695 days, 1.69 at T=1394. (3) The threshold stays 0.95; its meaning changes with the formula, so the operator re-signs it with the bars file. Today's formula (z against the cross-trial spread, trial mean added to E_max) stays only on the legacy path until D-043. ## Conflicts found between sources

- **`verdict_interpreter`'s fate** is stated three different ways across the corpus, in
  chronological order: (1) `delivery_plan_v26.md:315` "Corrected 2026-09-20 (operator decision)":
  removed from `STAGE_CONFIGS` outright (D-019); (2) `E-046b/S1_FINDINGS.md:287` "Part 5 operator
  decision": "delete it" (D-010); (3) `E-046a/S1_FINDINGS_5B_II.md:649-652` "Decision (operator,
  2026-09-23)": "bypass, not delete — keep the entry registered, make it unreached" (D-008),
  reconfirmed by the same file's REALIGNMENT section. **(3) is the decision that stands** — it is
  the latest, most specific, and matches the code (`STAGE_CONFIGS["verdict_interpreter"]` is
  still present and registered). (1) and (2) are recorded above as superseded, not deleted.
  `review_2026-09-27/A1_slices_4-5.md` (row 5b.4, finding 4) independently flags that the plan's
  own prose was never annotated to reflect this correction.
- **The FAIL/INCONCLUSIVE tie-break's confirmation status** is inconsistent across docs written
  the same day it was reportedly confirmed: `C1` in `DELIVERY_REVIEW.md` (2026-09-27) still
  describes it as "labelled OPERATOR-CONFIRMED in code, but its record says it was an overnight
  autonomous call awaiting Jeremy's confirmation" — i.e. as of the review, still unconfirmed in
  writing. This session's operator confirmation (D-014) resolves that gap going forward, but the
  review document itself predates it and should not be read as already reflecting D-014.

## Decisions pending

- **G10 and G13 resolved** by D-041 and D-042 (2026-09-28).
- **First real run:** protocol decided (D-050: the C1.7 template's generated BTC+ETH 1h layout);
  still pending: the operator's explicit go (`delivery_plan_v26_continuation.md` C4).
- **Model per stage** after the first real runs' cost is measured (today every stage runs on
  `claude-haiku-5-5` since D-135; before that, `claude-haiku-4-5`).
- **Tie-break doc note:** `DELIVERY_REVIEW.md` C1 predates D-014 and should be read with it.
- **Fold shape: contiguous folds with one continuous backtest, or today's interleaved blocks**
  (D-107 ruling 5, written 2026-10-12; **decided after lane 1's fold-B runs return; nothing is
  built**). Why it is open: lane 1's fold-A label "cost binds" came from window-end forced
  closes, which exist because every 4-month block is a separate backtest (D-107).
  - **Today, interleaved blocks:** `config/folds.yaml` holds three folds of six exact 4-month
    blocks; `tools/research_folds.py:148` `load_folds` refuses anything else
    (`BLOCKS_PER_FOLD = 6`, `:62`). Fold A happens to be contiguous (2022-01..2023-12); B and C
    interleave 2018-2021 so each has a block in every year (D-085, D-099).
    `tools/run_protocol.py:2558-2571` runs one backtest per coin and block, each warmed up
    (`warmup_prefetch`) and forced flat at its end (`trading-bot/core/trading_bot.py:642`, `_close_all_positions_at_end` `:648`); the grid reducers read one value per block
    (`tools/verdict_criteria_evaluator.py:1089`, including `sign_consistent_by_era`, `:1174`;
    blocks under `_SPARSE_TRADE_FLOOR` = 5 trades get no Sharpe, `run_protocol.py:55`); the
    whole-test chain (`tools/portfolio_whole_test.py:391` `chain_windows`) links the blocks,
    flat across the calendar gaps (`kind` "gap", `:602`). **Keeps:** era coverage per fold
    (B and C each see 2018, 2019, 2020 and 2021), many restarts. **Loses:** continuity: a hold
    longer than a block is cut at its end (lane 1: 21-30 window-end lots per run on fold A), a
    first-day fill's costs fall outside the chain (CUL-431 finding), and blocks too short for a
    slow signal's trade count.
  - **Contiguous folds, one continuous backtest per coin and fold, per-period numbers sliced
    from that one curve:** touches `config/folds.yaml` and `load_folds`' six-tile rule (a fold
    becomes one span, with slice labels); `run_phase1_research._protocol_windows_from_constraints`
    and its pre-flight mirror `run_campaign._expected_generated_protocol` (one window per fold);
    `run_protocol.py`'s window loop (one backtest, slices computed after it); the grid reducers
    (per-block values become slices of one curve; the sparse-trade floor per slice); the
    whole-test chain (one segment per coin, no junctions); decide-next's fold-use rule (a fold
    is used when any window overlaps it, D-085); **fold confirmation** (`tools/fold_confirm.py`:
    the run's windows must equal the fold's blocks exactly, `:507-512`; the sign must hold in all
    but one of at least `MIN_WINDOWS` = 4 windows, `:109-110`, with a false-confirm rate stated
    for 6 independent windows, `:119-120`; slices of one continuous backtest are not independent
    restarts, so that rate changes); the criterion window floors (`config/criterion_menu.yaml`
    `min_windows: 5`, lines 57 and 96, enforced at `tools/verdict_criteria_evaluator.py:1244-1246`);
    the era of a window = the era of its test period's midpoint (`tools/variant_coin.py:236`, so
    a two-year window has one era); the per-window data check of a fold (`variant_coin.window_coverage`,
    `decide_next._fold_data_reason`, "at least 4 windows with data", D-085), which becomes
    all-or-nothing per coin; and the novelty key's window fingerprint and the trial rows' `fold`
    field (`run_phase1_research._trial_fold_field`), so the repeat gate keys change. **Keeps:** continuity (no forced closes inside
    a fold, one warm-up). **Loses:** era coverage per fold: two calendar years per fold (for
    example 2018-2019 and 2020-2021, if carved from the same research period; how to carve them
    is part of this decision), so one fold can sit in one market phase; and direct
    comparability with every run since run_065 (blocks).
  - Not part of this item: the profit bars' thresholds (`cost_edge_ratio_min`,
    `trade_count_min`, CUL-431 note) and the chain's first-day anchor (CUL-431 finding).
